 ## 1. Scope and motivation
@@ -283,3 +283,64 @@ unchanged hidden-row envelopes, and fixed-seed campaigns with no new failure
 signatures. The census and oracle summaries are in
 `snapback4/reports/wave11-qdiag/`; WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md records the final checks
 and independent root Snapback 1 environment preconditions.
+
+## 9. Wave 12 declaration safety and diagnostic fidelity
+
+Quarry's binder-supplied expression inputs are reserved at every binding
+declaration. `viewer`, `now`, `commit` and `new` cannot name an operation or
+helper parameter, a local, or a loop variable. Refusal at the declaration is
+E_QUARRY_RESERVED with a concrete rename. This is a front-end safety boundary:
+the builtins keep their existing meaning and type, and no runtime lookup,
+shadowing rule, IR operation or wire value changes. The reservation prevents a
+valid-looking declaration from being ignored when expression binding resolves
+one of these names intrinsically.
+
+`use identity`, `use identity password` and `use identity guests` declare one
+project-wide method set. A second declaration is E_QUARRY_SYNTAX at the later
+site, whether it is in the same file or another file. The refusal names both
+method sets and the first site. It never merges or replaces methods, so source
+order and file order cannot silently change the authentication surface.
+
+Diagnostics recognize two SQL habits before generic shape lookup:
+`count table where predicate` says aggregates take parentheses and emits
+`count(table where predicate)`; a trailing `desc` says `last N by .column` is
+already descending and removes the suffix. Tests apply each emitted text to
+the diagnosed source and compile the result. Positional keys whose static type
+is a row/reference from another table lead with that mismatch (`secrets`
+cannot key `rolls`) before population-scan advice. A bad `search` field is a
+fault of that field: retain the table and its valid columns in the diagnostic
+catalog, omit only the unusable search entry, and avoid cascading unknown-table
+errors. This recovery affects rejected-source diagnostics only.
+
+A concrete named-update source rewrite always binds both `old` and `next`.
+When the rejected unnamed rule already says `old.field`, preserve it; map a
+bare `.field` in that same rule to `next.field`. The golden catalogue applies
+the whole rewrite and recompiles, including this mixed-image case. A
+multi-line `require` lookup skips layout tokens when finding its following
+`else CODE`, so its one-line repair keeps the author's exact code.
+
+E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT leads with a direct `.principalColumn = viewer` predicate
+when one read-rule disjunct makes that the smallest qualifying repair. The
+heavier point, maintained-total and constraint designs remain after it.
+N_UNREADABLE_PROBE names a prior readable dependency on the require/read path
+when one exists and states when implication is still unproved. Fixture
+argument drift is silent for every authored refusal expectation: a concrete
+code and the generic `denied`, `refused` and `failed` forms may intentionally
+carry invalid arguments. Malformed case structure and non-refusal expectations
+still receive N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT. The sweep summary calls derived
+require failures "probe refusals on derived arguments (expected)".
+
+A maintain may record an intentional aggregate delta channel with
+`disclosure reviewed 'rationale'`. The rationale must be nonempty, changes no
+read/write authority or runtime schema, and suppresses N_MAINTAIN_DELTA only
+for that maintain. Without it, the note offers the annotation alongside the
+existing restriction choices. N_DELETE_RESTRICTED points directly to the
+`On delete restrict` guide section, which distinguishes keep, cascade, set-null
+and restrict behavior across multi-level chains.
+
+Acceptance covers each declaration entry, same-file and cross-file identity,
+both SQL repairs, cross-table keys, invalid-search recovery, mixed old/next
+rewrites, multi-line refusal codes, direct viewer advice, require/read context,
+reviewed disclosure, every refusal expectation, derived-refusal output and the
+delete-guide pointer. These changes preserve execution, authorization,
+admission, completeness, storage and protocol semantics.

+/// An authored equality may already be the right authorization scope while a
+/// named order index leaves it as a residual filter. Print the two exact edits
+/// together: the compatible declaration and the same query selecting it.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — equality-filter advice is mechanically coherent even when it remains advisory.
+fn equality_filter_index_rewrite(table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table, scan: &snapback4_core::ir::Scan,
+    unpinned: &[String], leading: &[String], declaration: Option<&str>) -> Option<String> {
+    use snapback4_core::ir::{BinaryOp, Expr};
+    fn pins(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
+        match expr {
+            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And, left, right } => pins(left, column) || pins(right, column),
+            Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left, right } => field(left, column) && independent(right)
+                || field(right, column) && independent(left),
+            _ => false,
+        }
+    }
+    fn field(expr: &Expr, column: &str) -> bool {
+        matches!(expr, Expr::Field { base, name } if name == column
+            && matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter"))
+    }
+    fn independent(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
+        match expr {
+            Expr::Field { base, .. } => !matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(binding) if binding == "$filter") && independent(base),
+            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => independent(left) && independent(right),
+            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => independent(value),
+            Expr::Array(values) => values.iter().all(independent),
+            Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().all(independent),
+            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().all(independent),
+            Expr::Get(_) | Expr::Exists(_) | Expr::Scan(_) | Expr::Merge(_) | Expr::Block { .. } => false,
+            _ => true,
+        }
+    }
+    let filter = scan.filter.as_deref()?;
+    if unpinned.is_empty() || leading.len() > 8 || !unpinned.iter().all(|column| pins(filter, column)) { return None; }
+    let title = |value: &str| { let mut chars = value.chars(); chars.next()
+        .map(|first| first.to_uppercase().collect::<String>() + chars.as_str()).unwrap_or_default() };
+    let stem = leading.iter().filter(|column| column.as_str() != "id").map(|column| title(column)).collect::<String>();
+    if stem.is_empty() { return None; }
+    let mut name = format!("by{stem}");
+    let mut suffix = 2;
+    while table.indexes.contains_key(&name) {
+        name = format!("by{stem}{suffix}"); suffix += 1;
+    }
+    let declaration = declaration?;
+    let old = format!(" by {}", scan.index);
+    if !declaration.contains(&old) { return None; }
+    let rewritten = declaration.replacen(&old, &format!(" by {name}"), 1);
+    Some(format!("on table {} add `by {name}: {}`; use the same index in the rewritten query:\n```quarry\n{rewritten}\n```", scan.table, leading.join(", ")))
+}
+

+/// Pasteable named form for a rule that already names one image. Bare focal

+pub(crate) fn named_update_source(rule: &Expr) -> String {
+    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — preserve authored old/next images and map bare fields to next in named transitions.

+        Some(image) => format!("{image}.{name}"),
+        None => format!("next.{name}"),
+    });
+    format!("update (old, next) <- {body}")

 membership, and rank discloses relative values to the aggregate's read audience.
 The filter grants no source-row read. N_MAINTAIN_DELTA names dependencies;
 restrict the aggregate read rule when the delta is private (guide rules).
+When that delta is an intended, reviewed product disclosure, record the review
+without changing authority:
+
+```quarry
+maintain monthlyCounts: count escorts by zone, month(.at)
+  read <- exists staff[viewer]
+  disclosure reviewed 'staff intentionally see monthly zone totals'
+```
+
+The rationale silences N_MAINTAIN_DELTA for this maintain. It does not make the
+source or aggregate public and does not weaken either table's write rules.
 
 Even a hidden aggregate can disclose a constraint outcome: near an integer
 endpoint, a hidden selected contribution can make a write fail with
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/writes.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/writes.rs
index 890ecd0d8a..d7d675ba0b 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/writes.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/writes.rs
@@ -245,6 +245,24 @@ If the product instead permits readable availability checks, an `exists` or
 audience. Pinning a private guest merely narrows the check; it cannot enforce
 a constraint across all guests.
 
+## On delete restrict
+
+An undeclared reference is `on delete restrict`: deleting the parent refuses
+E_REFERENCED while any live child names it. Declare the product behavior on
+the child reference:
+
+- `on delete restrict` keeps audit/history rows and blocks the parent delete.
+- `on delete cascade` deletes children too; every parent group needs the
+  declared cap or unique key that bounds the cascade. A multi-level cascade
+  continues through each child's declared cascade references.
+- `on delete set null` clears an optional reference.
+- `on delete keep` preserves the child and its old parent id after deletion.
+
+For a multi-level ownership tree, use cascade only where deleting the root
+really deletes the descendant. Use keep or restrict at history and audit
+boundaries so deleting an intermediate row does not erase records that should
+survive.
+
 ## Server-arrival order within a conversation
 
 For timestamp order with ties, use existing columns: `sync last 500 by .at, .id`

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/programs.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/programs.rs
index c02b62b274..360071bebb 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/programs.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/programs.rs
@@ -6,8 +6,65 @@ use super::Parser;
 use crate::ast::*;
 use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, syntax, syntax_with, unsupported};
 use crate::lexer::Kind;
+use crate::print;
 
 impl Parser {
+    pub(super) fn parse_require(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
+        let site = self.take("require")?.site;
+        let condition = self.expression(0, &["else"])?;
+        // @ref LLP 3000.036#14.5 — name an unsupported text-pattern
+        // operator before diagnosing a refusal code the author already wrote.
+        let pattern = match &condition {
+            Expr::ShapeUse { base, name, site }
+                if super::TEXT_PATTERN_WORDS.contains(&name.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str()) =>
+            {
+                Some((name.clone(), print::expression(base), site.clone()))
+            }
+            _ => {
+                let next = self.peek();
+                (next.kind == Kind::Word
+                    && super::TEXT_PATTERN_WORDS.contains(&next.value.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str()))
+                .then(|| (next.value.clone(), print::expression(&condition), next.site.clone()))
+            }
+        };
+        if let Some((word, operand, at)) = pattern {
+            return Err(syntax_with(
+                &at,
+                format!("Quarry has no `{word}` operator; `{operand} {word} …` is not a condition"),
+                format!("compare whole text with `=` or `!=`; bound its length in the type (`{operand}: text 3..80`); declare a link `text <=200 format url` (http or https; anything else refuses E_INPUT before the program runs) or a handle `format handle`; or check the pattern in the client"),
+            )
+            .guide("types"));
+        }
+        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — a multiline require refusal preserves the author's code in its repair.
+        if self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) {
+            let mut at = 1;
+            while matches!(self.peek_at(at).kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Indent | Kind::Dedent) {
+                at += 1;
+            }
+            if self.peek_at(at).value == "else" {
+                let code = self.peek_at(at + 1).value.clone();
+                return Err(syntax_with(
+                    &site,
+                    "a require's else must be on the condition's closing line",
+                    format!("put `else {code}` on the closing line: `require {} else {code}`", print::expression(&condition)),
+                )
+                .guide("programs"));
+            }
+        }
+        if !self.at("else") {
+            return Err(syntax_with(
+                &site,
+                "require needs a refusal code: require <condition> else CODE",
+                format!("require {} else NOT_ALLOWED", print::expression(&condition)),
+            ));
+        }
+        self.take("else")?;
+        let code = self.word()?.value;
+        let message = if self.at_kind(Kind::Str) { Some(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value) } else { None };
+        self.newline()?;
+        Ok(Stmt::Require { condition, code, message, site })
+    }
+
     /// `schedule name every <duration> [at HH:MM]: job`, at least one minute,
     /// or `every N month[s]|year[s] [at [day D] HH:MM]`.
     pub(super) fn parse_schedule(&mut self) -> Fallible<ScheduleDecl> {

index bd731c7ea8..9a8de4786e 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/reserved.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/reserved.rs
@@ -11,6 +11,7 @@ pub(crate) const TABLE_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
 
 /// Words a statement or expression reads as syntax, so a parameter, local or
 /// loop variable of that name cannot be used (or silently means the keyword).
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — commit/new/now/viewer are contextual builtins, so declarations cannot shadow them.
 pub(crate) const BINDING_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
     "and", "as", "break", "commit", "continue", "created", "delete", "device", "elif", "else", "emit",
     "exists", "false", "for", "if", "insert", "merge", "new", "not", "now", "null", "oblivious", "or",
@@ -60,6 +61,28 @@ pub(crate) fn binding(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
         format!("rename it, for example `{name}`, at this declaration and at each use (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
 }
 
+pub(crate) fn repeated_identity(
+    site: &Site,
+    first: (bool, bool),
+    first_site: &Site,
+    repeated: (bool, bool),
+) -> Diagnostic {
+    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — identity is project-wide and repeated declarations must name both method sets.
+    let methods = |(password, guests)| match (password, guests) {
+        (true, true) => "password and guests",
+        (true, false) => "password",
+        (false, true) => "guests",
+        (false, false) => "no methods",
+    };
+    Diagnostic::new(
+        site,
+        "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
+        format!("`use identity` is project-wide: {} already declared at {first_site}; this repeated declaration names {}", methods(first), methods(repeated)),
+        "keep one declaration: `use identity` (password and guests), `use identity password`, or `use identity guests`",
+    )
+    .guide("production")
+}
+
 fn usage(word: &str) -> &'static str {
     match word {
         "read" | "insert" | "update" | "delete" => "read <- rule, insert <- rule, update <- rule, delete <- rule",

index 7906f0d1c8..fcfda49b0e 100644

+
+fn diagnostic(source: &str, code: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Diagnostic {
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    bound
+        .diagnostics
+        .iter()
+        .find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == code)
+        .cloned()
+        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing {code}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics))
+}
+
+fn replace_and_compile(source: &str, authored: &str, replacement: &str) {
+    let repaired = source.replacen(authored, replacement, 1);
+    assert_ne!(repaired, source, "fixture does not contain {authored:?}");
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn sql_habits_name_quarry_order_and_emit_compiling_rewrites() {
+    let count = "table logs:\n  kind: text <=20\n  public 'logs'\nquery total():\n  return count logs where .kind = 'error'\n";
+    let d = diagnostic(count, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
+    assert!(d.message.contains("aggregates take parentheses"), "{d}");
+    replace_and_compile(count, "count logs where .kind = 'error'", &d.rewrite);
+
+    let descending = "table logs:\n  at: time\n  public 'logs'\nquery recent():\n  return logs last 20 by .at desc\n";
+    let d = diagnostic(descending, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
+    assert!(d.message.contains("last 20 by .at") && d.message.contains("descending"), "{d}");
+    replace_and_compile(descending, "logs last 20 by .at desc", &d.rewrite);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn cross_table_identity_names_the_wrong_reference_before_scan_advice() {
+    let source = "table secrets:\n  public 'secrets'\ntable rolls:\n  public 'rolls'\nquery wrong(secret: secrets):\n  r = secrets[secret] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return exists rolls[r.id]\n";
+    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
+    assert!(d.message.contains("`r.id` is a secrets reference"), "{d}");
+    assert!(d.message.contains("rolls is keyed by its own id"), "{d}");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn invalid_search_keeps_the_table_symbol_for_later_references() {
+    let source = "table businesses:\n  category: int\n  search category\n  public 'businesses'\nquery one(id: businesses):\n  return businesses[id]\n";
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.message.contains("search category requires a text or enum column on businesses")), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.message.contains("unknown table businesses")), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn multiline_require_rewrite_preserves_the_authored_refusal_code() {
+    let source = "query choose(a: bool, b: bool):\n  require (a or\n    b)\n    else NOPE\n  return 1\n";
+    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
+    assert!(d.rewrite.contains("else NOPE"), "{d}");
+    assert!(!d.rewrite.contains("NOT_ALLOWED"), "{d}");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn reviewed_disclosure_silences_only_the_maintain_delta_note() {
+    let source = "table events:\n  owner: principal\n  category: text <=20\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  update <- allow\nmaintain totals: count events by category\n  read <- allow\n";
+    let warned = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(warned.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_MAINTAIN_DELTA"), "{:#?}", warned.notes);
+    let reviewed = compile(&[(
+        "schema.q".into(),
+        source.replace("  read <- allow\n", "  read <- allow\n  disclosure reviewed 'product staff intentionally see category totals'\n"),
+    )]);
+    assert!(reviewed.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", reviewed.diagnostics);
+    assert!(reviewed.notes.iter().all(|note| note.code != "N_MAINTAIN_DELTA"), "{:#?}", reviewed.notes);
+
+    for line in [
+        "  disclosure reviewed ''\n",
+        "  disclosure reviewed 'first review'\n  disclosure reviewed 'second review'\n",
+    ] {
+        let rejected = compile(&[(
+            "schema.q".into(),
+            source.replace("  read <- allow\n", &format!("  read <- allow\n{line}")),
+        )]);
+        assert!(rejected.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX"), "{:#?}", rejected.diagnostics);
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn structural_fanout_leads_with_a_direct_viewer_predicate() {
+    let source = "table bottles:\n  organizer: principal\n  read <- .organizer = viewer\n  update <- .organizer = viewer\ntable shares:\n  bottle: bottles\n  member: principal\n  organizer: principal\n  cap 120 by bottle\n  read <- .member = viewer or .organizer = viewer\n  update <- .organizer = viewer\nmutation cancel(bottle: bottles):\n  b = bottles[bottle] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require b.organizer = viewer else NOT_ORGANIZER\n  for share in shares where .bottle = bottle:\n    update shares[share.id] { member: share.member }\n";
+    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT");
+    assert!(d.rewrite.starts_with("First add `.organizer = viewer`"), "{d}");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn unreadable_probe_names_an_existing_require_read_path() {
+    let source = "table notes:\n  author: principal\n  read <- .author = viewer\ntable gatecodes:\n  note: notes\n  unique note\n  read <- exists notes[.note, author = viewer]\nmutation setCode(note: notes):\n  row = notes[note] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require row.author = viewer else NOT_AUTHOR\n  if exists gatecodes[note]:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n";
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    let note = bound.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE")
+        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", bound.notes));
+    assert!(note.message.contains("require/read path already established a readable notes row"), "{note}");
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn delete_restrict_note_opens_the_matching_guide_section() {
+    let source = "table parents:\n  read <- allow\n  delete <- allow\ntable children:\n  parent: parents\n  read <- allow\nmutation drop(parent: parents):\n  delete parents[parent]\n";
+    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    let note = bound.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED")
+        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", bound.notes));
+    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("guide writes --section \"On delete restrict\""), "{note}");
+}

+
+    let partially_named = "table fees:\n  owner: principal\n  status: enum('pending', 'archived')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- .owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived'\n  online only\n";
+    let diagnostic = refusal(partially_named, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
+    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
+    assert_eq!(

+        Some("update (old, next) <- ((next.owner = viewer) and (old.status != 'archived'))")
+    );

+        partially_named,
+        "update <- .owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived'",
+        &diagnostic,
+    );
+    assert!(repaired.contains("update (old, next)") && repaired.contains("next.owner"));

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
simple party pages pinning their leading order column. PAGE4's general positive
classifier bypassed the existing simple classifier's system/party order check.
Apply that same physical-order requirement to the independent compound model:
the authored native scan stays supported and incomplete, including empty and
nullable-order cases; do not hide the cases behind an Unsupported label.
Two model regressions retain readable rows and unknown absence. No runtime,
compiler plan, read authority or device bytes change. Re-run the complete 20k
campaign and formal checks after this model correction.

### 2026-10-14 — FIX28: pre-wave defects in unpublished 0.4.28

every supported frontend/template pair, twice; require their complete matrices
in imported Linux evidence. Browser execution belongs to the Linux host; clean

use snapback4_lang::compile;

#[test]
fn project_builtins_are_reserved_for_parameters_locals_and_loop_bindings() {
    for (source, name) in [
        ("query q(now: time):\n  return now\n", "now"),
        ("mutation m():\n  commit = now\n  return commit\n", "commit"),
        ("mutation m():\n  for new in range(0, 1):\n    return new\n", "new"),
        ("query q(viewer: principal):\n  return viewer\n", "viewer"),
    ] {
        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        let diagnostic = bound
            .diagnostics
            .iter()
            .find(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_RESERVED")
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{name}: {:?}", bound.diagnostics));
        assert!(diagnostic.message.contains(&format!("`{name}`")), "{diagnostic}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn repeated_identity_declarations_name_both_method_sets() {
    let same_file = compile(&[(
        "schema.q".into(),
        "use identity password\nuse identity guests\n".into(),
    )]);
    assert_eq!(same_file.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", same_file.diagnostics);
    let diagnostic = &same_file.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.code, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.site.line, 2);
    assert!(
        diagnostic.message.contains("password") && diagnostic.message.contains("guests"),
        "{diagnostic}"
    );

    let across_files = compile(&[
        ("schema.q".into(), "use identity guests\n".into()),
        ("accounts.q".into(), "use identity\n".into()),
    ]);
    assert_eq!(across_files.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", across_files.diagnostics);
    let diagnostic = &across_files.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.site.file, "accounts.q");
    assert!(
        diagnostic.message.contains("guests")
            && diagnostic.message.contains("password and guests"),
        "{diagnostic}"
    );
}

fn diagnostic(source: &str, code: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Diagnostic {
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    bound
        .diagnostics
        .iter()
        .find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == code)
        .cloned()
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing {code}: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics))
}

fn replace_and_compile(source: &str, authored: &str, replacement: &str) {
    let repaired = source.replacen(authored, replacement, 1);
    assert_ne!(repaired, source, "fixture does not contain {authored:?}");
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn sql_habits_name_quarry_order_and_emit_compiling_rewrites() {
    let count = "table logs:\n  kind: text <=20\n  public 'logs'\nquery total():\n  return count logs where .kind = 'error'\n";
    let d = diagnostic(count, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert!(d.message.contains("aggregates take parentheses"), "{d}");
    replace_and_compile(count, "count logs where .kind = 'error'", &d.rewrite);

    let descending = "table logs:\n  at: time\n  public 'logs'\nquery recent():\n  return logs last 20 by .at desc\n";
    let d = diagnostic(descending, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert!(d.message.contains("last 20 by .at") && d.message.contains("descending"), "{d}");
    replace_and_compile(descending, "logs last 20 by .at desc", &d.rewrite);
}

#[test]
fn cross_table_identity_names_the_wrong_reference_before_scan_advice() {
    let source = "table secrets:\n  public 'secrets'\ntable rolls:\n  public 'rolls'\nquery wrong(secret: secrets):\n  r = secrets[secret] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return exists rolls[r.id]\n";
    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    assert!(d.message.contains("`r.id` is a secrets reference"), "{d}");
    assert!(d.message.contains("rolls is keyed by its own id"), "{d}");
}

#[test]
fn invalid_search_keeps_the_table_symbol_for_later_references() {
    let source = "table businesses:\n  category: int\n  search category\n  public 'businesses'\nquery one(id: businesses):\n  return businesses[id]\n";
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.message.contains("search category requires a text or enum column on businesses")), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    assert!(!bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.message.contains("unknown table businesses")), "{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn multiline_require_rewrite_preserves_the_authored_refusal_code() {
    let source = "query choose(a: bool, b: bool):\n  require (a or\n    b)\n    else NOPE\n  return 1\n";
    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX");
    assert!(d.rewrite.contains("else NOPE"), "{d}");
    assert!(!d.rewrite.contains("NOT_ALLOWED"), "{d}");
}

#[test]
fn reviewed_disclosure_silences_only_the_maintain_delta_note() {
    let source = "table events:\n  owner: principal\n  category: text <=20\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  update <- allow\nmaintain totals: count events by category\n  read <- allow\n";
    let warned = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    assert!(warned.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_MAINTAIN_DELTA"), "{:#?}", warned.notes);
    let reviewed = compile(&[(
        "schema.q".into(),
        source.replace("  read <- allow\n", "  read <- allow\n  disclosure reviewed 'product staff intentionally see category totals'\n"),
    )]);
    assert!(reviewed.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:#?}", reviewed.diagnostics);
    assert!(reviewed.notes.iter().all(|note| note.code != "N_MAINTAIN_DELTA"), "{:#?}", reviewed.notes);

    for line in [
        "  disclosure reviewed ''\n",
        "  disclosure reviewed 'first review'\n  disclosure reviewed 'second review'\n",
    ] {
        let rejected = compile(&[(
            "schema.q".into(),
            source.replace("  read <- allow\n", &format!("  read <- allow\n{line}")),
        )]);
        assert!(rejected.diagnostics.iter().any(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX"), "{:#?}", rejected.diagnostics);
    }
}

#[test]
fn structural_fanout_leads_with_a_direct_viewer_predicate() {
    let source = "table bottles:\n  organizer: principal\n  read <- .organizer = viewer\n  update <- .organizer = viewer\ntable shares:\n  bottle: bottles\n  member: principal\n  organizer: principal\n  cap 120 by bottle\n  read <- .member = viewer or .organizer = viewer\n  update <- .organizer = viewer\nmutation cancel(bottle: bottles):\n  b = bottles[bottle] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require b.organizer = viewer else NOT_ORGANIZER\n  for share in shares where .bottle = bottle:\n    update shares[share.id] { member: share.member }\n";
    let d = diagnostic(source, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT");
    assert!(d.rewrite.starts_with("First add `.organizer = viewer`"), "{d}");
}

#[test]
fn unreadable_probe_names_an_existing_require_read_path() {
    let source = "table notes:\n  author: principal\n  read <- .author = viewer\ntable gatecodes:\n  note: notes\n  unique note\n  read <- exists notes[.note, author = viewer]\nmutation setCode(note: notes):\n  row = notes[note] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require row.author = viewer else NOT_AUTHOR\n  if exists gatecodes[note]:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n";
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    let note = bound.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE")
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", bound.notes));
    assert!(note.message.contains("require/read path already established a readable notes row"), "{note}");
}

#[test]
fn delete_restrict_note_opens_the_matching_guide_section() {
    let source = "table parents:\n  read <- allow\n  delete <- allow\ntable children:\n  parent: parents\n  read <- allow\nmutation drop(parent: parents):\n  delete parents[parent]\n";
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    let note = bound.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED")
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:#?}", bound.notes));
    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("guide writes --section \"On delete restrict\""), "{note}");
}

//! Read-only production and fixture authoring notes; never an execution gate.
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
use snapback4_lang::{bind::Bound, Diagnostic, Site};
use snapback4_core::{ir::{self, ProgramKind, ArgType}, schema::{RuleExpr, RuleTerm}, engine::validate, wire};
use crate::project::Project;

// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#8 — positive bootstrap dependencies form a least fixed point.
pub(super) fn notes(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, fixture: Option<&String>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let mut reachable = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut system = BTreeSet::new();
    let mut writers = BTreeSet::new();
    for p in &bound.programs {
        for (table, effect) in ir::write_tables(&p.program) {
            if matches!(effect, "insert" | "upsert") {
                writers.insert(table.clone());
                if p.program.kind == ProgramKind::Job { system.insert(table); }
            }
        }
        // Native jobs are opaque to the authoring analysis; no absence claim.
        if p.program.native { return fixture_notes(project, bound, fixture); }
    }
    loop {
        let previous = reachable.len();
        for (name, table) in bound.schema.visible_tables() {
            if bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some() { reachable.insert(name.to_owned()); continue; }
            if !writers.contains(name) { continue; }
            let refs_ready = table.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|through| reachable.contains(&through.parent));
            if refs_ready && (system.contains(name) || possible(&table.rules.insert, &reachable)) { reachable.insert(name.to_owned()); }
        }
        if reachable.len() == previous { break; }
    }
    let sources = project.sources().unwrap_or_default();
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    let blocked: BTreeSet<String> = bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, table)|
        table.ephemeral.is_none() && bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none() && !reachable.contains(name.as_str()))
        .map(|(name, _)| name.clone()).collect();
    let site = |name: &str| sources.iter().find_map(|(file, source)| source.lines().enumerate()
        .find(|(_, line)| line.trim_start().starts_with(&format!("table {name}:")))
        .map(|(line, _)| Site::new(file, line as u32 + 1, 1)))
        .unwrap_or_else(|| Site::new(&format!("table {name}"), 1, 1));
    if !bound.personas.is_empty() && !bound.schema.auth.password && !bound.schema.auth.guests {
        {
            let at = blocked.first().map(|name| site(name)).unwrap_or_else(|| Site::new("schema", 1, 1));
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&at, "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER",
                format!("persona-only: safe to ignore without `use identity`; no production identity{}", if blocked.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; review bootstrap for {}", blocked.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) }),
                "safe to ignore without `use identity`; when moving to production, add use identity password, map roles and relationships to account principals, and provision the first admin; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin").guide("deploy"));
        }
    } else {
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#3 — root components explain blocked descendants once.
        let graph: BTreeMap<String, BTreeSet<String>> = blocked.iter().map(|name| {
            let table = bound.schema.table(name).unwrap();
            let mut deps = BTreeSet::new();
            if writers.contains(name) {
                dependencies(&table.rules.insert, &mut deps);
                if let Some(through) = &table.through { deps.insert(through.parent.clone()); }
            }
            deps.retain(|dep| blocked.contains(dep));
            (name.clone(), deps)
        }).collect();
        let closure = |name: &str| {
            let mut seen = BTreeSet::from([name.to_owned()]);
            loop {
                let next: BTreeSet<_> = seen.iter().flat_map(|name| graph[name].iter().cloned()).collect();
                let count = seen.len(); seen.extend(next);
                if seen.len() == count { break; }
            }
            seen
        };
        let paths: BTreeMap<_, _> = blocked.iter().map(|name| (name.clone(), closure(name))).collect();
        for root in &blocked {
            let component: BTreeSet<_> = paths[root].iter().filter(|dep| paths[*dep].contains(root)).cloned().collect();
            if component.first() != Some(root) || paths[root].iter().any(|dep| !component.contains(dep)) { continue; }
            let dependents: Vec<_> = blocked.iter().filter(|name| !component.contains(*name) && paths[*name].contains(root)).cloned().collect();
            let cycle = if component.len() > 1 { format!(" (bootstrap cycle: {})", component.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) } else { String::new() };
            let dependent = if dependents.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; {} depend on it", dependents.join(", ")) };
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site(root), "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER",
                format!("{root}: no production writer can bootstrap this root{cycle}{dependent}; fixtures and /seed are dev-only"),
                bootstrap_recipe(bound, root)).guide("deploy"));
        }
    }
    notes.extend(fixture_notes(project, bound, fixture));
    notes
}
fn bootstrap_recipe(bound: &Bound, table: &str) -> String {
    let writers: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().filter(|p| ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(target,effect)| target==table && matches!(*effect,"insert"|"upsert"))).map(|p|p.program.name.clone()).collect();
    let missing = if writers.is_empty() { format!("missing mutation path: no mutation inserts {table}") }
        else { format!("missing service-seat bootstrap path through {} (their {table} insert rule depends on rows not yet provisioned)",writers.join(", ")) };
    format!("{missing}; add a bootstrap{table} mutation that requires `viewer = 'service:ops'`, allow that branch in {table}'s insert rule, and make its required probes readable to the seat. Mint `snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment`, then call the mutation with the real account principal and its bearer token. For synced rosters use the independent seat-or-member branch; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin")
}
fn possible(rule: &RuleExpr, rows: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Deny => false,
        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, .. } => rows.contains(table),
        RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|item| possible(item, rows)),
        RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|item| possible(item, rows)),
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => {
            if let (RuleTerm::Literal(a), RuleTerm::Literal(b)) = (left, right) { return a == b; }
            let principal = match (left, right) { (RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(v)) | (RuleTerm::Literal(v), RuleTerm::Viewer) => v.as_str().ok(), _ => None };
            if let Some(name) = principal.and_then(|p| p.strip_prefix("service:")) {
                return !name.is_empty() && name.len() <= 64 && name.bytes().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == b'_' || c == b'-');
            }
            if principal.is_some_and(|p| p.starts_with("dev:")) { return false; }
            left.references().chain(right.references()).all(|(table, _)| rows.contains(table))
        }
        // Unknown predicates are potentially satisfiable: this lint is not a theorem of reachability.
        RuleExpr::Ne(_, _) | RuleExpr::Compare(_, _, _) | RuleExpr::Not(_) => true,
    }
}
fn dependencies(rule: &RuleExpr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, .. } => { out.insert(table.clone()); }
        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => for item in items { dependencies(item, out); },
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => out.extend(left.references().chain(right.references()).map(|(table, _)| table.to_owned())),
        _ => {}
    }
}
fn fixture_notes(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, fixture: Option<&String>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let path = fixture.map(std::path::PathBuf::from).unwrap_or_else(|| project.root.join("fixtures")).join(super::test_cases::FILE);
    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(&path) else { return vec![]; };
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    for (index, line) in text.lines().enumerate() {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { continue; }
        let case: Result<serde_json::Value, _> = serde_json::from_str(line);
        // Shared cases may include operations added after the schema being checked.
        if case.as_ref().ok().and_then(|case| case["op"].as_str())
            .is_some_and(|op| !bound.programs.iter().any(|p| p.program.name == op)) { continue; }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — any authored refusal case owns its deliberately invalid arguments.
        let expects_refusal = case.as_ref().ok().is_some_and(|case| {
            case["expect"].as_str().is_some_and(|expect| {
                !matches!(expect, "ok" | "complete" | "capped" | "sent")
                    && case["op"].as_str().is_some_and(|op| bound.programs.iter().any(|p| p.program.name == op))
            })
        });
        let check = || -> Result<(), String> {
            let case = case.as_ref().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let op = case["op"].as_str().ok_or("case needs op")?;
            let program = &bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op).ok_or_else(|| format!("unknown operation {op}"))?.program;
            let empty = serde_json::json!({});
            let args = case.get("args").unwrap_or(&empty).as_object().ok_or("args must be an object")?;
            for (name, kind) in &program.args {
                if !args.contains_key(name) && !kind.is_optional() && !matches!(kind, ArgType::Cursor) { return Err(format!("{op}: missing argument {name}")); }
            }
            for (name, value) in args {
                let kind = program.args.get(name).ok_or_else(|| format!("{op} takes no argument {name}"))?;
                // Saved answers are runtime values; never claim a type failure for one.
                if saved(value) { continue; }
                let value = wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|e| format!("{name}: {e}"))?;
                validate::validate_arg(&bound.schema, kind, &value, name).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            }
            Ok(())
        };
        if let Err(reason) = check() {
            // An authored refusal expectation may deliberately supply values
            // outside the operation's accepted input domain.
            if expects_refusal { continue; }
            notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&Site::new(&path.to_string_lossy(), index as u32 + 1, 1), "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT", reason,
                "update this case's arguments to the operation's current signature; run snapback4 test").guide("testing"));
        }
    }
    notes
}
fn saved(value: &serde_json::Value) -> bool {
    match value { serde_json::Value::String(s) => s.starts_with('$') && s.len() > 1, serde_json::Value::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), serde_json::Value::Object(items) => items.values().any(saved), _ => false }
}

//! Reserved words: Quarry keywords cannot name a column or a binding. The
//! refusal names the word at its declaration, before a later use fails.

use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site};

/// Words that begin a table line (`by`, `read <-`, `after insert`, …).
pub(crate) const TABLE_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "after", "by", "cap", "delete", "deliver", "ephemeral", "expire", "immutable", "insert", "online",
    "public", "read", "retain", "search", "sync", "unique", "update",
];

/// Words a statement or expression reads as syntax, so a parameter, local or
/// loop variable of that name cannot be used (or silently means the keyword).
// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — commit/new/now/viewer are contextual builtins, so declarations cannot shadow them.
pub(crate) const BINDING_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "and", "as", "break", "commit", "continue", "created", "delete", "device", "elif", "else", "emit",
    "exists", "false", "for", "if", "insert", "merge", "new", "not", "now", "null", "oblivious", "or",
    "require", "return", "true", "update", "upsert", "viewer", "walk", "while",
];

fn rename(word: &str) -> String {
    match word {
        "read" => "isRead".into(),
        "by" => "author".into(),
        "after" => "afterAt".into(),
        "update" => "change".into(),
        "delete" => "removal".into(),
        "insert" => "addition".into(),
        "public" => "isPublic".into(),
        "unique" => "isUnique".into(),
        "sync" => "synced".into(),
        "cap" => "capacity".into(),
        "expire" => "expiresAt".into(),
        "not" => "startsAt".into(),
        "online" => "isOnline".into(),
        "search" => "searchText".into(),
        "retain" => "retained".into(),
        "immutable" => "locked".into(),
        "ephemeral" => "transient".into(),
        "deliver" => "delivery".into(),
        "walk" => "outing".into(),
        "viewer" => "who".into(),
        "now" => "at".into(),
        "commit" => "committedAt".into(),
        "new" => "newId".into(),
        other => format!("{other}Value"),
    }
}

pub(crate) fn column(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word: it begins a table line (`{}`), so it cannot name a column", usage(word)),
        format!("rename the column, for example `{name}:`, and use `.{name}` in rules and queries (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn binding(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word, so it cannot name a parameter, local or loop variable"),
        format!("rename it, for example `{name}`, at this declaration and at each use (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn repeated_identity(
    site: &Site,
    first: (bool, bool),
    first_site: &Site,
    repeated: (bool, bool),
) -> Diagnostic {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — identity is project-wide and repeated declarations must name both method sets.
    let methods = |(password, guests)| match (password, guests) {
        (true, true) => "password and guests",
        (true, false) => "password",
        (false, true) => "guests",
        (false, false) => "no methods",
    };
    Diagnostic::new(
        site,
        "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
        format!("`use identity` is project-wide: {} already declared at {first_site}; this repeated declaration names {}", methods(first), methods(repeated)),
        "keep one declaration: `use identity` (password and guests), `use identity password`, or `use identity guests`",
    )
    .guide("production")
}

fn usage(word: &str) -> &'static str {
    match word {
        "read" | "insert" | "update" | "delete" => "read <- rule, insert <- rule, update <- rule, delete <- rule",
        "by" | "unique" => "by name: columns, unique columns",
        "after" => "after insert <- exists …",
        "public" => "public 'why'",
        "sync" | "online" => "sync … / online only",
        "cap" => "cap N by column",
        "expire" => "expire at .column",
        "not" => "not before .column",
        "immutable" => "immutable columns",
        "retain" => "retain until-revoked",
        "ephemeral" => "ephemeral 5s",
        "search" => "search column",
        _ => "a table line",
    }
}
