//! `test.cases.jsonl` beside the fixture tables: the application's own
//! operations, in order, as named viewers, each with the outcome it expects.
//! One line is `{"as": "alice", "op": "addCard", "args": {..}, "expect": "sent"}`;
//! `expect` is `sent` (a mutation's default), `complete`, `capped`, `ok` (any
//! answer), or a refusal code (`E_RULE`, `NOT_FOUND`, a program's own `require`
//! code). A query's default is its `check` verdict: `complete`, or either
//! answer when `check` notes the query is never complete (N_NEVER_COMPLETE).
//! Optional `data` must equal the answer; `count` is the answer's length; `save`
//! names the answer so a later string argument `"$name"` or `"$name.field"`
//! reuses it. `as` is a declared persona, as on dev: an undeclared one is
//! refused E_AUTH.
use crate::project::persona_principal;
use snapback4_core::engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace;
use snapback4_core::ir::ProgramKind;
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Refusal, WriteState, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::path::Path;

pub const FILE: &str = "test.cases.jsonl";

/// Dev's cast check, for `test` and a local `run`: with `use personas`
/// declared, a bare name (or `dev:<name>`) outside the cast is refused as dev
/// refuses its header (E_AUTH); a principal id such as `user:…` passes as itself.
pub fn undeclared_persona(name: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Option<String> {
    if declared.is_empty() { return None; }
    let bare = match name.split_once(':') { None => name, Some(("dev", bare)) => bare, Some(_) => return None };
    (!declared.iter().any(|p| p == bare)).then(|| format!(
        "persona {bare} is not declared; `use personas {}` is this project's cast, and dev refuses {bare} with E_AUTH (401). Add {bare} to it, or use a declared persona (an outsider is a declared persona with no rows)",
        declared.join(", ")))
}

/// The persona a case without `as` runs as: alice when she is in the cast (or
/// there is none), else the first declared persona.
pub fn default_persona(declared: &[String]) -> &str {
    if declared.is_empty() || declared.iter().any(|p| p == "alice") { "alice" } else { &declared[0] }
}

/// One case as `test`'s query sweep reads it: which query, as whom, with
/// which arguments (`None` when they name a saved answer, which only the case
/// run has), expecting what.
pub struct Explicit { pub at: String, pub op: String, pub principal: String, pub args: Option<Json>, pub expect: Option<String> }

/// The cases file read leniently, before the sweep: a line that does not
/// parse is left to [`run`], which refuses it with its line number.
// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.2 — wave 7: the sweep runs a persona's own case arguments and labels a refusal its case expects.
pub fn explicit(fixture: &str, declared: &[String]) -> Vec<Explicit> {
    let Ok(text) = std::fs::read_to_string(Path::new(fixture).join(FILE)) else { return Vec::new() };
    fn saved(value: &Json) -> bool {
        match value { Json::String(text) => text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1, Json::Array(items) => items.iter().any(saved), Json::Object(fields) => fields.len() == 1 && fields.contains_key("$literal") || fields.values().any(saved), _ => false }
    }
    text.lines().enumerate().filter_map(|(index, line)| {
        let line = line.trim();
        if line.is_empty() || line.starts_with("//") { return None; }
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).ok()?;
        let op = case.get("op")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(declared));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        let args = Some(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({}))).filter(|args| args.is_object() && !saved(args));
        Some(Explicit { at: format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1), op, principal, args, expect: case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str).map(str::to_owned) })
    }).collect()
}

/// Runs every case; prints one line each and returns (passed, failed).
/// `sources` are the compiled files, to quote a refusing clause's line.
pub fn run(engine: &mut Engine, bound: &Bound, fixture: &str, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<(usize, usize), String> {
    let path = Path::new(fixture).join(FILE);
    let text = match std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
        Ok(text) => text,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok((0, 0)),
        Err(error) => return Err(format!("cannot read {}: {error}", path.display())),
    };
    let mut saved = BTreeMap::<String, Json>::new();
    let (mut passed, mut failed) = (0, 0);
    let mut verdicts_shown = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    let lines: Vec<(usize, &str)> = text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty() && !line.trim_start().starts_with("//")).collect();
    // Every line is checked before any runs: a misspelled key must not pass
    // silently (wave 5: `"rows":0` passed against one row), and a typo on the
    // last line should not wait for every earlier case.
    let mut cases = Vec::with_capacity(lines.len());
    for (index, line) in &lines {
        let at = format!("{FILE}:{}", index + 1);
        let case: Json = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
        validate(&case).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        cases.push((at, case));
    }
    println!("test: cases from {FILE}");
    for (at, case) in cases {
        let Some(op) = case.get("op").and_then(Json::as_str) else { return Err(format!("{at}: each case names \"op\" (a query or mutation)")) };
        let Some(bound_program) = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op) else {
            let starter = super::init::starter_fixture(FILE).is_some_and(|starter| starter == text);
            let hint = if starter { format!("\n  hint: {FILE} is the todo starter's unmodified cases for list/add/toggle/remove; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it (write your own cases, `snapback4 guide testing`)") } else { String::new() };
            return Err(format!("{at}: unknown operation {op}{hint}"));
        };
        let program = bound_program.program.clone();
        let persona = case.get("as").and_then(Json::as_str).unwrap_or_else(|| default_persona(&bound.personas));
        let principal = if persona.contains(':') { persona.to_owned() } else { persona_principal(persona) };
        // A query's default expectation is check's verdict: a noted never-complete page passes capped.
        let never_complete = (program.kind == ProgramKind::Query).then(|| never_complete(bound, bound_program)).flatten();
        let explicit = case.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str);
        let expect = explicit.unwrap_or(match program.kind { ProgramKind::Query if never_complete.is_some() => "ok", ProgramKind::Query => "complete", _ => "sent" });
        let label = case.get("label").and_then(Json::as_str).map(|l| format!(" — {l}")).unwrap_or_default();
        let raw_args = substitute(case.get("args").cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| json!({})), &saved).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
        let input = (|| -> Result<_, Refusal> {
            if let Some(why)=crate::persona_principals::arguments(&bound.personas,&program.args,&raw_args) { return Err(why); }
            let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
            let fields = raw_args.as_object().ok_or_else(|| Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "args must be an object"))?;
            for (name, value) in fields {
                let kind = program.args.get(name).ok_or_else(|| Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{op} takes no argument {name}")))?;
                args.insert(name.clone(), wire::from_json(kind, value).map_err(|why| Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {name}: {why}")))?);
            }
            Ok(args)
        })();
        let input_refusal = input.as_ref().err().cloned();
        let args = input.unwrap_or_default();
        let trace = RefusalTrace::default();
        let context_viewer = principal.clone();
        let context = Context::new(principal, args).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone());
        // (outcome word, answer) or a refusal
        let mut unavailable = false;
        let got: Result<(&str, Json), Refusal> = if let Some(refusal) = input_refusal { Err(refusal) }
        else if let Some(why) = undeclared_persona(persona, &bound.personas) {
            Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", why))
        } else { match program.kind {
            ProgramKind::Query => match engine.query(op, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => { unavailable = outcome.unavailable; Ok((if outcome.complete { "complete" } else { "capped" }, wire::to_json(&outcome.data))) },
                Err(refusal) => Err(refusal),
            },
            ProgramKind::Job => return Err(format!("{at}: {op} is a job; cases run queries and mutations")),
            _ => match engine.mutate(op, None, context) {
                Ok(outcome) => match outcome.state {
                    WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => Ok(("sent", wire::to_json(&result))),
                    WriteState::Failed { why, .. } => Err(why),
                },
                Err(error) => Err(error.refusal()),
            },
        } };
        let mut problems = Vec::new();
        let mut notes = Vec::new();
        let shown = match &got {
            Ok((word, answer)) => {
                let admitted = *word == expect || expect == "ok" || (expect == "complete" && *word == "sent");
                if !admitted { problems.push(if expect == "capped" && *word == "complete" {
                    "now complete — update the case: replace expect: \"capped\" with expect: \"complete\" (or omit expect); readable pinned-parent guards can now certify this page".to_owned()
                } else { format!("expected {expect}, got {word}") }); }
                if *word == "capped" && let Some(note) = &never_complete {
                    if admitted && explicit.is_none() && verdicts_shown.insert(op.to_owned()) { notes.push(format!("capped is this query's check verdict: {note}")); }
                    if !admitted { notes.push(format!("hint: {note}; a capped answer still holds every row you may read: add \"expect\":\"capped\" (or drop \"expect\"), or pin the page as the note's rewrite says (snapback4 check --verbose)")); }
                }
                if let Some(data) = case.get("data") && data != answer {
                    problems.push(format!("data differs: got {answer}"));
                    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.12 — an unavailable answer is not a null value: say why it has none.
                    if unavailable { notes.push("null here is no answer (a client shows `loading`), not a null value: a ceiling cut it, or an `exists` over rows this viewer only partly reads found no row it may read, which cannot prove absence (snapback4 check --verbose)".into()); }
                }
                if let Some(count) = case.get("count").and_then(Json::as_u64) {
                    let length = answer.as_array().map(|a| a.len()).or_else(|| answer.get("items").and_then(Json::as_array).map(|a| a.len()));
                    if length != Some(count as usize) { problems.push(format!("expected {count} rows, got {}", length.map(|n| n.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| "a non-list".into()))); }
                }
                if let Some(name) = case.get("save").and_then(Json::as_str) { saved.insert(name.to_owned(), answer.clone()); }
                (*word).to_owned()
            }
            Err(refusal) => {
                let code = &refusal.code;
                if code != expect && !(expect == "denied" || expect == "refused" || expect == "failed") {
                    problems.push(format!("expected {expect}, got {code}: {}", refusal.message));
                    notes.extend(refusal_evidence(&trace, refusal, bound, sources, explicit, &context_viewer));
                }
                code.clone()
            }
        };
        if problems.is_empty() {
            passed += 1;
            println!("      ok   {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}");
            if let Err(refusal) = &got && refusal.code == "E_INPUT" { println!("           {}", refusal.message); }
        } else {
            failed += 1;
            println!("      FAIL {at} {op} ({persona}) → {shown}{label}: {}", problems.join("; "));
        }
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query && trace.hidden() > 0 { println!("           hidden: {} (encountered row images filtered by read rules)", trace.hidden()); }
        for note in notes { println!("           {note}"); }
    }
    Ok((passed, failed))
}

/// The keys a case line may carry.
pub const KEYS: [&str; 8] = ["as", "op", "args", "expect", "data", "count", "save", "label"];

// @ref LLP 3000.036#15.3 — an unknown key is refused, never ignored: ignoring it was a false green.
fn validate(case: &Json) -> Result<(), String> {
    let Json::Object(fields) = case else { return Err("each case is a JSON object: {\"op\":..,\"as\":..,\"args\":{..},\"expect\":..}".into()) };
    let valid = KEYS.iter().map(|key| format!("`{key}`")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
    for key in fields.keys() {
        if KEYS.contains(&key.as_str()) { continue; }
        let meant = match key.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str() {
            "rows" | "length" | "len" | "size" | "n" | "total" | "counts" => Some("count"),
            "arguments" | "params" | "parameters" | "input" | "inputs" | "arg" => Some("args"),
            "persona" | "viewer" | "user" | "who" | "principal" => Some("as"),
            "operation" | "query" | "mutation" | "name" => Some("op"),
            "result" | "answer" | "value" | "rowsequal" | "equals" => Some("data"),
            "expected" | "expects" | "outcome" | "state" | "code" | "status" => Some("expect"),
            "description" | "desc" | "comment" | "note" | "title" => Some("label"),
            _ => None,
        };
        let hint = meant.map(|key| format!(" (did you mean `{key}`?)")).unwrap_or_default();
        return Err(format!("unknown key `{key}`{hint}; a case takes only {valid} (snapback4 guide testing)"));
    }
    for key in ["as", "op", "expect", "save", "label"] {
        if let Some(value) = fields.get(key) && !value.is_string() {
            let hint = if key == "expect" && value.is_object() { "; compare the answer with `data` (the whole answer) or `count` (its length) beside `expect`" } else { "" };
            return Err(format!("`{key}` is a string, got {value}{hint}"));
        }
    }
    if let Some(name) = fields.get("save").and_then(Json::as_str) && (name.is_empty() || name.contains('.') || name.starts_with('$')) {
        return Err(format!("save alias {name:?} must be nonempty with no dot or leading dollar; use a name such as r1, then reference $r1 or $r1.field"));
    }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("count") && value.as_u64().is_none() { return Err(format!("`count` is a non-negative integer, got {value}")); }
    if let Some(value) = fields.get("args") && !value.is_object() && fields.get("expect").and_then(Json::as_str) != Some("E_INPUT") { return Err("\"args\" is an object".into()); }
    Ok(())
}

/// The query's N_NEVER_COMPLETE note, as `N_NEVER_COMPLETE at schema.q:L`.
fn never_complete(bound: &Bound, program: &snapback4_lang::BoundProgram) -> Option<String> {
    bound.notes.iter().chain(&program.notes)
        .find(|note| matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE") && note.site == program.site)
        .map(|note| format!("{}: {} ({} at {}:{})", program.program.name, note.message, note.code, note.site.file, note.site.line))
}

/// Development evidence under a failing refusal: which layer refused, at which
/// line (LLP 3000.036 §3 B; the refusal itself never names the clause).
fn refusal_evidence(trace: &RefusalTrace, refusal: &Refusal, bound: &Bound, sources: &[(String, String)], explicit: Option<&str>, viewer: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    let lookup = |file: &str| sources.iter().find(|(name, _)| name == file).map(|(_, text)| text.clone());
    let cause = trace.take();
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    match cause {
        Some(cause) if cause.terminal => {
            let record = super::run_refusal::record(&cause, &bound.refusal_sites);
            if let Some(text) = super::run_refusal::explain(&record, &lookup) { out.push(text.trim_start_matches("dev: ").to_owned()); }
            // Keep the operation source explanation alongside private table-rule evidence.
            if refusal.code == "NOT_FOUND" {
                if let Some(site) = refusal.site.as_deref() {
                    let resolved = operation_site(bound, site);
                    if let Some(line) = super::run_refusal::quote(resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(site), &lookup) {
                        if !line.contains("else NOT_FOUND") {
                            let at = resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(site).trim_end_matches(":1");
                            out.push(format!("NOT_FOUND is the write target's answer at `{line}` ({at}): the row is missing, or this viewer cannot read it and the table's write rule refused (a hidden row answers NOT_FOUND, never E_RULE; guide refusals)"));
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
            if refusal.code == "E_RULE" && (cause.clause.starts_with("unique ") || cause.clause.starts_with("cap ")) {
                let held = if cause.clause.starts_with("unique ") { format!("a row with this `{}` key exists that this viewer cannot read", cause.clause) } else { format!("the `{}` group holds rows this viewer cannot read", cause.clause) };
                out.push(format!("hint: {held}; your own code would disclose it, so the engine refuses E_RULE: expect E_RULE here, or make those rows readable to the viewer (guide rules)"));
            }
        }
        // A program's own `! else CODE` or `require … else CODE`, at its
        // source line. When that site is a `t[x] !` lookup and t's read rule
        // hid a row in this operation, the row exists but reads as absent to
        // this viewer: a later `require` never ran.
        cause => if let Some(site) = refusal.site.as_deref().filter(|_| refusal.rule.as_deref() == Some(refusal.code.as_str())) {
            // An engine write-target refusal carries an operation-relative site
            // (`removeSale:2`); resolve it to the source line the program wrote.
            let resolved = operation_site(bound, site);
            let line = super::run_refusal::quote(resolved.as_deref().unwrap_or(site), &lookup);
            let shown = match (&line, &resolved) {
                (Some(line), Some(at)) => format!("`{line}` ({})", at.trim_end_matches(":1")),
                (Some(line), None) => format!("`{line}` ({site})"),
                (None, _) => site.to_owned(),
            };
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — wave 5: a NOT_FOUND the program never names is the write target's answer, not "the program's own refusal".
            if refusal.code == "NOT_FOUND" && !line.as_ref().is_some_and(|line| line.contains("else NOT_FOUND")) {
                out.push(format!("NOT_FOUND is the write target's answer at {shown}: the row is missing, or this viewer cannot read it and the table's write rule refused (a hidden row answers NOT_FOUND, never E_RULE; guide refusals)"));
                return out;
            }
            let hidden = cause.filter(|cause| !cause.terminal && cause.phase == "read rule"
                && bound.refusal_sites.contains_key(&(cause.table.clone(), format!("lookup {site}"))));
            match hidden {
                Some(cause) => {
                    let rule = bound.refusal_sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "read rule".into()));
                    let quoted = rule.map(|rule| match super::run_refusal::quote(rule, &lookup) { Some(text) => format!("`{text}` ({rule})"), None => rule.clone() }).unwrap_or_else(|| "its read rule".into());
                    let later = explicit.filter(|code| *code != refusal.code && sources.iter().any(|(_, text)| text.contains(&format!("else {code}")))).map(|code| format!("; a later `require … else {code}` never ran")).unwrap_or_default();
                    out.push(format!("{} is the program's own refusal at {shown}: the {} read rule {quoted} hid a row: hidden from {viewer} by {}'s read rule; a hidden row reads as absent to a lookup{later}", refusal.code, cause.table, cause.table));
                    out.push("hint: no code after the lookup can tell \"not yours\" from \"missing\": expect NOT_FOUND here, admit the viewer in the read rule, or write by id with no lookup so the table's update/delete rule decides (snapback4 guide testing, guide refusals)".into());
                }
                None => out.push(format!("{} is the program's own refusal at {shown}", refusal.code)),
            }
        },
    }
    out
}

/// `op:N` (line N of an operation, its header line 1) as `file:line:1`.
fn operation_site(bound: &Bound, site: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let (op, line) = site.rsplit_once(':')?;
    let line: u32 = line.parse().ok()?;
    let program = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op)?;
    Some(format!("{}:{}:1", program.site.file, program.site.line + line.checked_sub(1)?))
}

/// `"$name"` is a saved answer (a saved row's id); `"$name.field"` (any depth) one of its fields.
fn substitute(value: Json, saved: &BTreeMap<String, Json>) -> Result<Json, String> {
    Ok(match value {
        Json::Object(mut fields) if fields.len() == 1 && fields.contains_key("$literal") => fields.remove("$literal").unwrap(),
        Json::String(text) if text.starts_with("$$") => Json::String(text[1..].to_owned()),
        Json::String(text) if text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1 => {
            let mut parts = text[1..].split('.');
            let name = parts.next().unwrap_or_default();
            let mut current = saved.get(name).ok_or_else(|| format!("{text}: nothing was saved as {name} (add \"save\": \"{name}\" to an earlier case)"))?;
            let mut fields = 0;
            for part in parts {
                current = current.get(part).ok_or_else(|| format!("{text}: the saved answer has no field {part}"))?;
                fields += 1;
            }
            // An argument names a row by its id: a bare `$name` for a saved
            // row (an insert's answer) means that row's id.
            match current.get("id").and_then(Json::as_str) { Some(id) if fields == 0 => Json::String(id.to_owned()), _ => current.clone() }
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#2 — a bare save alias refuses by name instead of becoming a silent literal id.
        Json::String(text) if saved.contains_key(&text) => return Err(format!("{text:?} names a saved answer; write \"${text}\" for its id (or \"${text}.field\" for a field)")),
        Json::Array(items) => Json::Array(items.into_iter().map(|item| substitute(item, saved)).collect::<Result<_, _>>()?),
        Json::Object(fields) => Json::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(k, v)| Ok((k, substitute(v, saved)?))).collect::<Result<_, String>>()?),
        other => other,
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod saved_alias_tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn aliases_resolve_or_refuse_by_name_and_literals_are_expressible() {
        for name in ["", "r.1", "$r1"] { assert!(validate(&json!({"op":"add", "save":name})).unwrap_err().contains("save alias")); }
        let saved = [("r1".into(), json!({"id":"real-row-id", "text":"hello", "nested":{"n":2}}))].into();
        assert_eq!(substitute(json!({"row":"$r1", "text":"$r1.text", "nested":["$r1.nested.n"]}), &saved).unwrap(), json!({"row":"real-row-id", "text":"hello", "nested":[2]}));
        let refused = substitute(json!({"row":"r1"}), &saved).unwrap_err();
        assert!(refused.contains("r1") && refused.contains("$r1"), "{refused}");
        assert!(substitute(json!("$missing"), &saved).unwrap_err().contains("nothing was saved as missing"));
        assert!(substitute(json!("$r1.absent"), &saved).unwrap_err().contains("no field absent"));
        assert_eq!(substitute(json!({"text":{"$literal":"r1"}, "money":"$$50", "ordinary":"price $50"}), &saved).unwrap(), json!({"text":"r1", "money":"$50", "ordinary":"price $50"}));
    }
}
use super::*;

#[test]
fn intentional_input_cases_and_later_operations_are_not_fixture_drift() {
    let p = Project::new();
    p.source("use personas alice\nquery answer(n: int 1..5):\n  return n\n");
    fs::create_dir(p.0.join("fixtures")).unwrap();
    let cases = [
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":"wrong"},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":2,"extra":1},"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":false,"expect":"E_INPUT"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"denied"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"refused"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"failed"}"#,
        r#"{"op":"answer","args":{"n":6},"expect":"NOT_ALLOWED"}"#,
    ].join("\n");
    fs::write(p.0.join("fixtures/test.cases.jsonl"), &cases).unwrap();
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
    assert!(!parsed(&out)["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().any(|n| n["code"] == "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT"));
    let test = p.run(&["test", "--fixture", "fixtures"]);
    assert!(test.status.success(), "{test:?}");
    fs::write(p.0.join("fixtures/test.cases.jsonl"), format!("{cases}\n{{\"op\":\"later\",\"args\":{{}}}}\n{{\"op\":\"answer\",\"args\":{{\"n\":6}},\"expect\":\"sent\"}}\n")).unwrap();
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    let json = parsed(&out);
    let notes: Vec<_> = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT").collect();
    assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{json}");
    let test = p.run(&["test", "--fixture", "fixtures"]);
    assert!(!test.status.success());
    let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&test.stderr);
    assert!(text.contains("unknown operation later"), "{test:?}");
    assert!(!p.store().exists());
}

#[test]
fn production_readiness_groups_dependents_and_collapses_persona_identity() {
    let p = Project::new();
    let source = "use identity password\ntable staff:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  insert <- exists staff[viewer]\n  online only\ntable rooms:\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- exists staff[viewer]\n  online only\ntable bookings:\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- exists staff[viewer]\n  online only\nmutation staffer(principal: principal):\n  return insert staff { principal }\nmutation room():\n  return insert rooms {}\nmutation booking():\n  return insert bookings {}\n";
    p.source(source);
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
    let json = parsed(&out);
    let notes: Vec<_> = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER").collect();
    assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{json}");
    let msg = notes[0]["message"].as_str().unwrap();
    assert!(msg.starts_with("staff:") && msg.contains("bookings, rooms depend on it"), "{msg}");
    p.source(&source.replace("use identity password", "use personas alice"));
    let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]);
    let json = parsed(&out);
    let notes: Vec<_> = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER").collect();
    assert_eq!(notes.len(), 1, "{json}");
    assert!(notes[0]["message"].as_str().unwrap().starts_with("persona-only: safe to ignore without `use identity`; no production identity"));
    for seat in ["ops", "not a seat", ""] {
        p.source(&source.replace("insert <- exists staff[viewer]", &format!("insert <- viewer = 'service:{seat}' or exists staff[viewer]")));
        let out = p.run(&["check", "--json"]); assert!(out.status.success(), "{out:?}");
        let json = parsed(&out);
        let count = json["notes"].as_array().unwrap().iter().filter(|n| n["code"] == "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER").count();
        assert_eq!(count, usize::from(seat != "ops"), "{seat}: {json}");
    }
}

#[test]
fn fixture_constraints_keep_the_column_and_code_instead_of_not_found() {
    let p = Project::new();
    p.source("table proofs:\n  version: int 1..\n  label: text <=30\n  unique label else DUPLICATE\n  public 'proofs'\nquery versions():\n  return proofs first 10 by .id\n");
    fs::create_dir(p.0.join("fixtures")).unwrap();
    for (rows, expected) in [
        (r#"{"id":"p","version":0,"label":"a"}"#, "proofs.version"),
        ("{\"id\":\"p1\",\"version\":1,\"label\":\"a\"}\n{\"id\":\"p2\",\"version\":1,\"label\":\"a\"}", "DUPLICATE"),
    ] {
        fs::write(p.0.join("fixtures/proofs.jsonl"), rows).unwrap();
        let out = p.run(&["test", "--fixture", "fixtures"]);
        assert!(!out.status.success());
        let text = format!("{}{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout), String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr));
        assert!(text.contains(expected) && !text.contains("NOT_FOUND"), "{text}");
        assert!(text.contains("E_CONSTRAINT") || text.contains("DUPLICATE"), "{text}");
    }
    assert!(!p.store().exists());
}

   254	                    self.diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_MODIFIER_SCOPE",
   255	                        format!("{modifier} could modify the inner or outer read; parenthesize the inner read, or move the modifier before `where`"),
   256	                        format!("({})", print::read_expr(read))).guide("reads").mechanical());
   257	                }
   258	                break;
   259	            }
   260	            // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — SQL-habit refusals carry pasteable Quarry rewrites.
   261	            // SQL writes `count table where …`; Quarry makes the counted
   262	            // domain an ordinary parenthesized expression.
   263	            if matches!(&value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name == "count")
   264	                && self.at_kind(Kind::Word)
   265	                && self.peek_at(1).value == "where"
   266	            {
   267	                let Expr::Name { site, .. } = &value else { unreachable!() };
   268	                let site = site.clone();
   269	                let table = self.word()?.value;
   270	                let read = ReadExpr {
   271	                    cursor_prefix: None,
   272	                    computed: Default::default(),
   273	                    order_keys: Vec::new(),
   274	                    table,
   275	                    keys: Vec::new(),
   276	                    index: None,
   277	                    range: None,
   278	                    matching: None,
   279	                    filter: None,
   280	                    seeks: Vec::new(),
   281	                    site: site.clone(),
   282	                };
   283	                let read = self.parse_range(read, self.position - 1, false)?;
   284	                return Err(syntax_with(
   285	                    &site,
   286	                    "aggregates take parentheses: `count(table where …)`",
   287	                    format!("count({})", print::read_expr(&read)),
   288	                )
   289	                .guide("from-sql"));
   290	            }
   291	            if self.at("?") && self.peek_at(1).value == "." {
   292	                let site = self.take("?")?.site;
   293	                self.take(".")?;
   294	                self.word()?;
   295	                // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.5 — ordinary field access is null-propagating and evaluates its base once.
   296	                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_QUARRY_UNSUPPORTED", "?. is not part of Snapback 4; field access already returns null for a null base", ".").mechanical());
   297	            } else if self.at(".") && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Word {
   298	                let site = self.take(".")?.site;
   299	                let name = self.word()?.value;
   300	                value = Expr::Member { base: Box::new(value), name, site };
   301	            } else if self.at("(") && call_name(&value).is_some() {
   302	                let (name, name_site) = call_name(&value).expect("a helper name");
   303	                let site = self.take("(")?.site;
   304	                if name == "count" && self.at("*") {
   305	                    let rewrite = self.count_star_rewrite();
   306	                    if self.peek_at(1).value == ")" { self.register_repair(&name_site, start..self.position + 2, &rewrite, None); }
   307	                    let mut refusal = unsupported(&name_site, "count(*)", rewrite).guide("from-sql");
   308	                    if refusal.rewrite.is_empty() { refusal.message.push_str("; pass the table or filtered read to count"); }
   309	                    self.position += 1;
   310	                    self.take(")")?;
   311	                    self.diagnostics.push(refusal);
   312	                    value = Expr::Call { name, args: Vec::new(), site };
   313	                    continue;
   314	                }
   315	                let mut args = Vec::new();
   316	                while !self.at(")") {
   317	                    args.push(if name == "bucket" && args.len() == 1 { self.maintain_duration(false)? }
   318	                        else if matches!(name.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket") && args.len() == if name == "bucket" { 2 } else { 1 } { self.maintain_duration(true)? }
   319	                        else { self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])? });
   320	                    if !self.maybe(",") {
   321	                        break;
   322	                    }
   323	                }
   324	                self.take(")")?;
   325	                value = Expr::Call { name, args, site };
   326	            } else if self.at("[") && matches!(value, Expr::Name { .. }) {
   327	                let Expr::Name { name, site } = value else { unreachable!() };
   328	                value = Expr::Read(self.parse_read(&name, &site)?);
   329	            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Name { .. }) && matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "first" | "after" | "last" | "matching" | "by" | "where") && !stops.contains(&self.peek().value.as_str()) {
   330	                // `t first N by index`: the whole table's page, the empty
   331	                // prefix spelled without brackets, as `chunk` takes it.
   332	                let Expr::Name { name, site } = value else { unreachable!() };
   333	                value = Expr::Read(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: name, keys: Vec::new(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site });
   334	            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Read(_)) && self.at("limit") && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Number {
   335	                let site = self.take("limit")?.site;
   336	                let n = self.number()?;
   337	                return Err(unsupported(&site, "limit", format!("first {n} — ascending; use last {n} for descending order")).guide("reads"));
   338	            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Read(_)) && self.at("before") {
   339	                return Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site.clone(), "before", "use after c; reverse display order in the client when needed"));
   340	            } else if matches!(value, Expr::Read(_)) && matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "matching" | "after" | "first" | "last" | "by" | "where") {
   341	                let Expr::Read(read) = value else { unreachable!() };
   342	                let read = self.parse_range(read, start, stops.contains(&"<probe-shape>"))?;
   343	                // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — `last` already expresses descending order; remove SQL's trailing marker.
   344	                if self.at("desc") {
   345	                    let desc = self.peek().clone();
   346	                    let rewrite = print::read_expr(&read);
   347	                    let page = read.range.as_ref().map_or_else(
   348	                        || "a Quarry page".to_owned(),
   349	                        |range| format!(
   350	                            "{} {} by {}",
   351	                            if range.order == Order::Desc { "last" } else { "first" },
   352	                            range.take,
   353	                            if read.order_keys.is_empty() {
   354	                                read.index.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| "<index>".into())
   355	                            } else {
   356	                                read.order_keys
   357	                                    .iter()
   358	                                    .map(|key| format!(".{key}"))
   359	                                    .collect::<Vec<_>>()
   360	                                    .join(", ")
   361	                            }
   362	                        ),
   363	                    );
   364	                    return Err(syntax_with(
   365	                        &desc.site,
   366	                        format!("`{page}` is already {}; Quarry has no trailing `desc`", if read.range.as_ref().is_some_and(|range| range.order == Order::Desc) { "descending" } else { "ascending" }),
   367	                        rewrite,
   368	                    )
   369	                    .guide("from-sql"));
   370	                }
   371	                value = Expr::Read(read);
   372	            } else if self.maybe("!") {
   373	                let assertion = self.tokens[self.position - 1].site.clone();
   374	                if !self.maybe("else") {
   375	                    return Err(syntax_with(&assertion, "presence assertions require a refusal code", format!("{} ! else NOT_FOUND", print::expression(&value))));
   376	                }
   377	                let code = self.word()?.value;
   378	                let message = if self.at_kind(Kind::Str) { Some(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value) } else { None };
   379	                value = Expr::Assert { value: Box::new(value), code, message, site: assertion };
   380	            } else if self.at("{") {
   381	                let site = self.take("{")?.site;
   382	                let table = expression_table(&value).map(str::to_owned).unwrap_or_default();
   383	                let fields = self.with_focal(&table, |parser| parser.parse_shape_fields())?;
   384	                self.take("}")?;
   385	                value = Expr::Shape { base: Box::new(value), fields, site };
   386	            } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && !stops.contains(&"<probe-shape>") && precedence(&self.peek().value).is_none() && !stops.contains(&self.peek().value.as_str()) && !matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "else" | "for" | "in" | "if" | "absent") {
   387	                let named = self.word()?;
   388	                value = Expr::ShapeUse { base: Box::new(value), name: named.value, site: named.site };
   389	            } else {
   390	                break;
//! Programs, chunks and schedules: `query`, `mutation`, `job` (Quarry
//! chunks or `native`, LLP 3000.005; a query may be `native` too, §9) and `schedule`. Split from the parser
//! so each file stays under the line budget.

use super::Parser;
use crate::ast::*;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, syntax, syntax_with, unsupported};
use crate::lexer::Kind;
use crate::print;

impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn parse_require(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
        let site = self.take("require")?.site;
        let condition = self.expression(0, &["else"])?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#14.5 — name an unsupported text-pattern
        // operator before diagnosing a refusal code the author already wrote.
        let pattern = match &condition {
            Expr::ShapeUse { base, name, site }
                if super::TEXT_PATTERN_WORDS.contains(&name.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str()) =>
            {
                Some((name.clone(), print::expression(base), site.clone()))
            }
            _ => {
                let next = self.peek();
                (next.kind == Kind::Word
                    && super::TEXT_PATTERN_WORDS.contains(&next.value.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str()))
                .then(|| (next.value.clone(), print::expression(&condition), next.site.clone()))
            }
        };
        if let Some((word, operand, at)) = pattern {
            return Err(syntax_with(
                &at,
                format!("Quarry has no `{word}` operator; `{operand} {word} …` is not a condition"),
                format!("compare whole text with `=` or `!=`; bound its length in the type (`{operand}: text 3..80`); declare a link `text <=200 format url` (http or https; anything else refuses E_INPUT before the program runs) or a handle `format handle`; or check the pattern in the client"),
            )
            .guide("types"));
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — a multiline require refusal preserves the author's code in its repair.
        if self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) {
            let mut at = 1;
            while matches!(self.peek_at(at).kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Indent | Kind::Dedent) {
                at += 1;
            }
            if self.peek_at(at).value == "else" {
                let code = self.peek_at(at + 1).value.clone();
                return Err(syntax_with(
                    &site,
                    "a require's else must be on the condition's closing line",
                    format!("put `else {code}` on the closing line: `require {} else {code}`", print::expression(&condition)),
                )
                .guide("programs"));
            }
        }
        if !self.at("else") {
            return Err(syntax_with(
                &site,
                "require needs a refusal code: require <condition> else CODE",
                format!("require {} else NOT_ALLOWED", print::expression(&condition)),
            ));
        }
        self.take("else")?;
        let code = self.word()?.value;
        let message = if self.at_kind(Kind::Str) { Some(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value) } else { None };
        self.newline()?;
        Ok(Stmt::Require { condition, code, message, site })
    }

    /// `schedule name every <duration> [at HH:MM]: job`, at least one minute,
    /// or `every N month[s]|year[s] [at [day D] HH:MM]`.
    pub(super) fn parse_schedule(&mut self) -> Fallible<ScheduleDecl> {
        let site = self.take("schedule")?.site;
        let rewrite = "schedule digest every 1d at 08:00: sendDigests";
        let name = self.binding_name()?;
        if !self.maybe("every") {
            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a schedule fires every so often: schedule name every 2m: job", rewrite));
        }
        let amount = self.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
        let unit = self.word()?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar months and years, UTC, anchored at 1970-01.
        let calendar = match unit.value.as_str() { "month" | "months" => Some(1), "year" | "years" => Some(12), _ => None };
        if let Some(per) = calendar {
            let rewrite = "schedule billing every 1 month at day 1 09:00: bill";
            let months = amount.value.parse::<u32>().ok().filter(|n| *n >= 1).and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(per))
                .filter(|n| *n <= snapback4_core::schema::CALENDAR_MONTHS_LIMIT)
                .ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&amount.site, "a calendar schedule fires every 1 to 1200 months (100 years)", rewrite).guide("jobs"))?;
            let mut offset_ms = 0;
            if self.maybe("at") {
                let mut day = 1;
                if self.maybe("day") {
                    let token = self.peek().clone();
                    day = self.number()?;
                    if !(1..=28).contains(&day) {
                        return Err(syntax_with(&token.site, format!("day {day} is not in every month; a calendar schedule's day is 1 through 28"),
                            "at day 28 09:00, or a daily schedule whose job tests month(periodStart) != month(periodEnd): that daily period crosses a month's end").guide("jobs"));
                    }
                }
                let hours = self.number()?;
                self.take(":")?;
                let minutes = self.number()?;
                if hours > 23 || minutes > 59 {
                    return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at takes a day of the month and a time of day: at day 1 09:00", rewrite));
                }
                offset_ms = (u64::from(day - 1) * 86_400 + u64::from(hours) * 3_600 + u64::from(minutes) * 60) * 1_000;
            }
            return self.schedule_tail(name, 0, offset_ms, months, site);
        }
        let multiplier = super::views::duration_unit(&unit.value).ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&unit.site,
            "a schedule period is a whole number followed by ms, s, m, h or d, or a number of months or years", "every 2m, every 1d, or every 1 month"))?;
        // Integer millisecond boundaries need no rounding to whole minutes.
        // Retain the range of previously accepted m/h/d schedule periods.
        let every_ms = amount.value.parse::<i64>().ok().and_then(|value| value.checked_mul(multiplier))
            .ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&amount.site, "schedule period exceeds the representable millisecond range", "every 1d"))? as u64;
        if every_ms < 60_000 {
            return Err(syntax_with(&amount.site, "a schedule fires at most once a minute", "every 1m"));
        }
        let mut offset_ms = 0;
        if self.maybe("at") {
            if self.at("day") {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at day D names a day of a calendar month; it needs every 1 month (or N months or years)", "schedule billing every 1 month at day 1 09:00: bill").guide("jobs"));
            }
            let hours = self.number()?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let minutes = self.number()?;
            if hours > 23 || minutes > 59 {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at takes a time of day, HH:MM", rewrite));
            }
            offset_ms = (u64::from(hours) * 60 + u64::from(minutes)) * 60_000;
            if offset_ms >= every_ms {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at names a time of day inside the period; it needs every 1d (or longer)", rewrite));
            }
        }
        self.schedule_tail(name, every_ms, offset_ms, 0, site)
    }

    fn schedule_tail(&mut self, name: String, every_ms: u64, offset_ms: u64, months: u32, site: crate::Site) -> Fallible<ScheduleDecl> {
        let service = if self.maybe("as") {
            let namespace = self.word()?;
            if namespace.value != "service" {
                return Err(syntax_with(&namespace.site, "a scheduled action runs as a service seat", "as service:billing").guide("actions"));
            }
            self.take(":")?;
            let mut name = self.service_name_part()?;
            while self.maybe("-") {
                name.push('-');
                name.push_str(&self.service_name_part()?);
            }
            Some(format!("service:{name}"))
        } else { None };
        self.take(":")?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#10 — module jobs use the same qualified name in schedules as in the compiled program.
        let mut target = self.word()?.value;
        if self.maybe(".") { target.push('.'); target.push_str(&self.word()?.value); }
        self.end_line(&site)?;
        Ok(ScheduleDecl { name, every_ms, offset_ms, target, service, months, site })
    }

    fn service_name_part(&mut self) -> Fallible<String> {
        if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { Ok(self.take_kind(Kind::Number)?.value) } else { Ok(self.word()?.value) }
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_program(&mut self) -> Fallible<ProgramDecl> {
        let kind_token = self.word()?;
        let kind = match kind_token.value.as_str() {
            "query" => ProgramKind::Query,
            "mutation" => ProgramKind::Mutation,
            _ => ProgramKind::Job,
        };
        let name = self.binding_name()?;
        if kind == ProgramKind::Job && self.at("every") {
            let mut period = String::new();
            let mut end = 0;
            while !matches!(self.peek().kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Eof) {
                let token = self.peek().clone();
                if !period.is_empty() && token.site.col > end { period.push(' '); }
                period.push_str(&token.value);
                end = token.site.col + token.value.len() as u32;
                self.position += 1;
            }
            let period = period.trim_end_matches(':').trim_end();
            let module = std::path::Path::new(&kind_token.site.file).file_stem().and_then(|stem| stem.to_str()).unwrap_or("schema");
            let target = if module == "schema" { name.clone() } else { format!("{module}.{name}") };
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(&kind_token.site, "E_QUARRY_JOB_SCHEDULE",
                format!("job {name} declares work; a separate schedule declares when it runs"),
                format!("job {name}():  -- keep the existing indented body\nschedule {name}Every {period}: {target}")).guide("jobs"));
        }
        if self.at(":") {
            return Err(syntax_with(&kind_token.site, "an operation with no arguments still needs parentheses", format!("{} {name}():", kind_token.value)).mechanical());
        }
        let args_site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let args = self.parse_args()?;
        self.repair_args = Some((args_site, args.clone()));
        if self.at("->") {
            return Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site.clone(), "result annotations", "results are inferred; remove the annotation"));
        }
        let held = kind == ProgramKind::Query && self.maybe("held");
        let predicts = kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && self.maybe("predicts");
        // A clause may continue the header on the body's indentation level.
        let continued = self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Indent && self.peek_at(2).value == "reports";
        if continued { self.newline()?; self.take_kind(Kind::Indent)?; }
        let delivery = if self.at("reports") { Some(self.parse_delivery()?) } else { None };
        if delivery.is_some() && !self.at(":") {
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(&self.peek().site, "E_DELIVERY_CLAUSE",
                "a mutation header has one delivery clause, followed by its body colon",
                "keep one reports delivery of messages by .conversation, .ordinal: clause").guide("receipts"));
        }
        self.take(":")?;
        if self.at("native") {
            // `job name(): native` — the body is the app's Rust (LLP 3000.005).
            let native = self.take("native")?;
            self.end_line(&native.site)?;
            if kind == ProgramKind::Mutation {
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&native.site, "E_NATIVE", format!("only a job or a query can be native; a mutation runs in the interpreter so a device can predict it and the server can replay it"), format!("job {name}(): native  -- or: query {name}(): native")).guide("native"));
            }
            return Ok(ProgramDecl { delivery, held, predicts, server: false, kind, name, args, prelude: Vec::new(), body: Vec::new(), native: true, site: kind_token.site });
        }
        let server = kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && self.maybe("server");
        self.newline()?;
        let before_body = self.diagnostics.len();
        let (prelude, body) = if kind == ProgramKind::Job { self.parse_chunks(&args)? } else { (Vec::new(), if continued { self.parse_block_contents()? } else { self.parse_block()? }) };
        if body.is_empty() {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.19 — default `check` hides a recovery diagnostic only
            // when an earlier error in the same operation's own body already explains it.
            // A job's parsed prelude cannot explain missing chunks; that repair is independent.
            if kind == ProgramKind::Job { return Err(job_without_sweep(&kind_token.site, &name, &prelude)); }
            let mut diagnostic = syntax(&kind_token.site, format!("{} {name} has an empty body", kind_token.value));
            diagnostic.cascade_of = self.diagnostics[before_body..].iter().find(|d| d.code.starts_with("E_")).map(|d| d.site.clone());
            return Err(diagnostic);
        }
        Ok(ProgramDecl { delivery, held, predicts, server, kind, name, args, prelude, body, native: false, site: kind_token.site })
    }

    fn parse_delivery(&mut self) -> Fallible<DeliveryDecl> {
        let site = self.take("reports")?.site;
        let parsed = (|| {
            self.take("delivery")?;
            self.take("of")?;
            let table = self.word()?.value;
            self.take("by")?;
            self.take(".")?;
            let group = self.word()?.value;
            self.take(",")?;
            self.take(".")?;
            let position = self.word()?.value;
            Ok(DeliveryDecl { table, group, position, site: site.clone() })
        })();
        parsed.map_err(|_: Diagnostic| Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_DELIVERY_CLAUSE",
            "a delivery clause names one table and its group and position columns",
            "mutation delivered(conversation: conversations, from: int, through: int) reports delivery of messages by .conversation, .ordinal:").guide("receipts"))
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_chunks(&mut self, args: &[ArgDecl]) -> Fallible<(Vec<Stmt>, Vec<Stmt>)> {
        self.take_kind(Kind::Indent)?;
        let mut prelude = Vec::new();
        let mut chunks = Vec::new();
        let mut locals: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> = args.iter().map(|arg| arg.name.clone()).collect();
        while !self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) && !self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
            self.skip_newlines();
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) {
                break;
            }
            if self.at("for") {
                let sweep = self.parse_for()?;
                let automatic = match &sweep {
                    Stmt::For { domain: Expr::Read(read), .. } => read.range.is_none(),
                    Stmt::For { domain: Expr::Name { name, .. }, .. } => !locals.contains(name),
                    _ => false,
                };
                if !automatic && chunks.is_empty() {
                    prelude.push(sweep);
                    continue;
                }
                if let Stmt::For { domain: Expr::Read(read), site, .. } = &sweep
                    && (read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some()) {
                    return Err(syntax_with(site, "an automatic sweep needs a plain table or prefix domain, without a page or matching clause", format!("for row in {}:  -- the engine chooses each batch", read.table)));
                }
                chunks.push(sweep);
                continue;
            }
            if !self.at("chunk") && chunks.is_empty() {
                let statement = self.parse_statement()?;
                if let Stmt::Bind { names, .. } = &statement { locals.extend(names.iter().cloned()); }
                prelude.push(statement);
                continue;
            }
            let site = self.take("chunk")?.site;
            let name = self.local_name()?;
            self.take("in")?;
            let start = self.position;
            let table = self.word()?;
            let mut domain = if self.at("[") { self.parse_read(&table.value, &table.site)? } else { ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: table.value.clone(), keys: Vec::new(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: table.site.clone() } };
            if !self.at("first") {
                return Err(syntax(&self.peek().site.clone(), "chunk requires first N by index"));
            }
            domain = self.parse_range(domain, start, false)?;
            self.document.source.record(&Expr::Read(domain.clone()), &self.source, &self.tokens[start..self.position]);
            if domain.index.is_none() || domain.range.as_ref().is_none_or(|r| r.order != Order::Asc || r.cursor.is_some()) {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "chunk requires table[prefix] first N by index"));
            }
            self.take(":")?;
            self.newline()?;
            let body = self.parse_block()?;
            if body.is_empty() {
                return Err(syntax(&site, "chunk body cannot be empty"));
            }
            chunks.push(Stmt::For { name, domain: Expr::Read(domain), body, site });
        }
        self.take_kind(Kind::Dedent)?;
        Ok((prelude, chunks))
    }
}

/// A job body is a sweep (`guide jobs`): name the loop to write, over the
/// table the prelude already writes or pages when there is one.
fn job_without_sweep(site: &crate::Site, name: &str, prelude: &[Stmt]) -> crate::Diagnostic {
    fn table(statements: &[Stmt]) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        statements.iter().find_map(|statement| match statement {
            Stmt::Expr { value: Expr::Write { table, .. }, .. } | Stmt::Bind { value: Expr::Write { table, .. }, .. } | Stmt::Return { value: Expr::Write { table, .. }, .. } => Some((table.as_str(), false)),
            Stmt::For { domain: Expr::Read(read), .. } => Some((read.table.as_str(), true)),
            Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => table(body),
            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => table(then_body).or_else(|| table(else_body)),
            _ => None,
        })
    }
    let found = table(prelude);
    let swept = found.map_or("<table>", |(table, _)| table);
    let seen = match found {
        Some((_, true)) => format!("; a paged `for … in {swept} first N` only reads ahead of a sweep"),
        Some(_) => "; its writes are outside any sweep".to_owned(),
        None => String::new(),
    };
    syntax_with(site, format!("job {name} has an empty body: a job body is a sweep, one top-level `for row in table:` loop with every write inside it{seen}"),
        format!("for row in {swept}:  -- then indent the writes under it, address the row as row.id and test mutable columns with `if` (or bound it: for row in {swept} where .<id or immutable column> >= periodStart by .<that column>:)")).guide("jobs")
}

//! A private population cannot answer a cross-row constraint for its writer.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#5.1 — aggregate consumers retain scan authorization and admission.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{engine::{admission, rules}, schema::{Constraint, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema}};

impl BoundProgram {
    /// The shortest repair for a hidden-row population when one direct read
    /// disjunct exposes the viewer-owned slice.
    pub(crate) fn direct_viewer_repair(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<String> {
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        let column = table.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().find_map(|rule| match rule {
            RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer)
            | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) => Some(column.as_str()),
            _ => None,
        })?;
        Some(format!(
            "First add `.{column} = viewer` to this read's `where` predicate; that directly selects the read-rule audience and is the smallest qualifying repair. "
        ))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.025#6-diagnostics-and-exact-documentation-changes — retain the admission repair before the qualified receipt-gap fallback.
    pub(crate) fn expiry_gap_diagnostic(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<Diagnostic> {
        use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        if !table.is_expiring() || !rules::scan_visibility(schema, scan).uniform { return None; }
        let scan_index = table.index(&scan.index)?;
        let ordinal = scan_index.components.get(scan.prefix.len())?.column()?;
        if table.columns.get(ordinal) != Some(&ColumnType::Int) || !scan.orders_field(schema, ordinal) { return None; }
        let (parent, parent_table) = table.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique).find_map(|index| {
            if index.components.len() != 2 { return None; }
            let columns = [index.components[0].column()?, index.components[1].column()?];
            let parent = if columns[0] == ordinal { columns[1] } else if columns[1] == ordinal { columns[0] } else { return None; };
            let ColumnType::Ref { table: parent_table } = table.columns.get(parent)? else { return None; };
            // Consider every covering key, in either column order. An earlier
            // key on an unpinned parent cannot hide the applicable one.
            let scope = scan_index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix)
                .find_map(|(part, key)| (part.column() == Some(parent)).then(|| key.scalar()).flatten())?;
            rules::prefix_visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &[(parent, scope)].into()).uniform
                .then_some((parent, parent_table))
        })?;
        // A sender/ordinal equality may make the refused page uniform while
        // other positions in the parent group remain hidden. Never call those holes.
        // Uniformity proves one verdict for the scope, not that it is readable;
        // proving a dominating caller check needs control-flow/rule implication.
        let name = &scan.table;
        // Quarry comments keep the whole rewrite pasteable without hiding the
        // smaller repair that admits the author's original scan.
        let rewrite = format!("-- {}\n-- Fallback if the mutation must keep its prediction or effect ordering:\nfn skipExpired_{name}(scope: {parent_table}, initialPrefix: int, low: int):\n  prefix = initialPrefix\n  for i in range(0, 1024):\n    if prefix + 1 >= low:\n      break\n    if exists {name}[{parent} = scope, {ordinal} = prefix + 1]:\n      break\n    prefix = prefix + 1\n  return prefix", problem.rewrite);
        Some(Diagnostic::new(&self.sources.get(site)?.0, problem.code,
            format!("{}. For receipt gaps, try the admission repair below first. If the shape must stay refused, use bounded unique ordinal probes; a new schema can declare `{ordinal}: sequence by {parent}` with `unique {parent}, {ordinal}`. The helper's prefix justifies advancing a receipt only after the caller's own authority check (the membership check or the card's readable-message anchor). Call it inside the receipt merge, retain disjoint runs, and let an already-recorded run reach the helper again to resume after the 1,024-probe limit. For an unread badge use a row-keyed count; for general processing use a query or job (guide sequences)", problem.message),
            rewrite).guide("sequences"))
    }

    /// This is diagnostic selection only. The ordinary admission pass still
    /// owns the capped-scope case; neither path changes a rule or a scan.
    pub(crate) fn constraint_diagnostic(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<Diagnostic> {
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        if !self.constraint_domains.contains(site) { return None; }
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        if rules::scan_visibility(schema, scan).uniform || capped_scope_decides(schema, scan) { return None; }
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        let source = &self.sources.get(site)?.0;
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — render the repairs against the author's own table and pins (app-farm patterns 6, 19).
        let pins: Vec<(String, String)> = table.index(&scan.index).map(|index| index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix)
            .filter_map(|(part, key)| {
                let column = part.column()?.clone();
                let value = match key.scalar()? { Expr::Variable(name) if !name.starts_with('$') => name.clone(), _ => column.clone() };
                Some((column, value))
            }).collect()).unwrap_or_default();
        let name = &scan.table;
        let group = pins.iter().map(|(column, _)| column.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let key = pins.iter().map(|(_, value)| value.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — offer the direct viewer predicate before heavier structural repairs.
        let direct_audience = self.direct_viewer_repair(schema, problem).unwrap_or_default();
        let example = format!("declare one row per reserved unit with `unique resource, unit else TAKEN`, then insert those rows inside `{}`", self.program.name);
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#16.2 — wave 6 R2: `require not exists children…` before a delete is a zero count, not a capacity.
        let by = pins.iter().map(|(column, _)| format!(".{column}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let absence = (!pins.is_empty()).then(|| absence_require(&self.program.body, site)).flatten().map(|code| format!(
            "an absence check over every {name} row, whoever may read it, is a zero count (it discloses only the count per {group}, never who): declare `view {name}Count: count {name} by {by}` with `read <- allow`, then in `{}` write `require {name}Count[{key}] = 0 else {code}` in place of the `not exists` (before a delete of the parent, this guard also silences N_DELETE_RESTRICTED; guide recipes, \"Booking\", `removeSlot`). Otherwise, ", self.program.name)).unwrap_or_default();
        Some(Diagnostic::new(source, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT",
            format!("`{}` can read only the {} its viewer may read (`{}`). A check over them would miss hidden {} rows, so it is refused. A point read of a full unique key (`exists {name}[…]`) is not refused, but it too sees only readable rows",
                self.program.name, scan.table, table.rules.read.quarry(), scan.table),
            format!("{direct_audience}{absence}for one known {name} row, use a point read by id (`{name}[id]`); for a global total, read a maintained aggregate whose explicit read rule admits this caller (public only if its totals may be public). use a constraint every write obeys, whoever wrote the clashing row: {example}; a clash rolls back the whole mutation. A hidden clash stays E_RULE; make the unit table public only if availability may be public, so a readable clash can return TAKEN. Or use a readable audience: check a public availability table if those rows may be public. {count}A `require` role guard does not make the population readable. See `snapback4 guide writes` for the per-night example", count = if pins.is_empty() || !absence.is_empty() { String::new() } else {
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#14.6 — wave 4 #15: the count view and the author's own capacity check, as code.
                let check = capacity_require(&self.program.body, site).map_or_else(|| format!("require {name}Count[{key}] < limit else FULL"),
                    |(op, bound, code)| format!("require {name}Count[{key}] {op} {bound} else {code}"));
                format!("For a count or capacity, keep a count whose read rule covers the writers (it discloses only the count per {group}, never who): `view {name}Count: count {name} by {by}` with `read <- allow`, then in `{}` `{check}` before the insert; the insert itself keeps the count current (guide indexes, \"Private lists\"). ", self.program.name)
            }))
            .guide("writes"))
    }
}

/// Keep the existing scope-pinning rewrite only when a declared bounded
/// group supplies enough deciding columns. Preserve the current pins and
/// ask the engine's rule analysis, including self-probe absorption, whether
/// pinning that scope gives one verdict. A cap on unrelated columns cannot
/// turn a private conflict check into a check over every writer's rows.
fn capped_scope_decides(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> bool {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { return false };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return false };
    let pinned: BTreeMap<String, Expr> = index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix)
        .filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar()).map(|(column, value)| (column.clone(), value.clone())))
        .collect();
    table.constraints.iter().any(|constraint| {
        let Constraint::Cap { by: group, max } = constraint else { return false };
        if u64::from(*max) > snapback4_core::Ceilings::default().examined { return false; }
        let mut candidate = pinned.clone();
        for column in group { candidate.entry(column.clone()).or_insert_with(|| ir::var("$constraint_scope")); }
        rules::prefix_visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &candidate.iter().collect()).uniform
    })
}

/// The `require count(page) OP bound else CODE` that consumes the refused
/// page, with `bound` printed when it is a local, a field of one, or a literal.
fn capacity_require(body: &[ir::Stmt], site: &str) -> Option<(&'static str, String, String)> {
    fn print(expr: &Expr) -> Option<String> {
        match expr {
            Expr::Variable(name) if !name.starts_with('$') => Some(name.clone()),
            // A local is renamed `$s2` for `s`; compiler temporaries are not locals.
            Expr::Variable(name) => {
                let local = name.strip_prefix('$')?.trim_end_matches(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit());
                (!local.is_empty() && !["present", "probe", "bool", "row", "tmp", "pureCall"].contains(&local)).then(|| local.to_owned())
            }
            Expr::Field { base, name } => Some(format!("{}.{name}", print(base)?)),
            Expr::Literal(Value::Int(n)) => Some(n.to_string()),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    let counts = |expr: &Expr| matches!(expr, Expr::Builtin { builtin: ir::Builtin::Count | ir::Builtin::CountCapped, args } if matches!(args.as_slice(), [Expr::Scan(scan)] if scan.site == site));
    body.iter().find_map(|statement| match statement {
        ir::Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Binary { op, left, right }, code, .. } if counts(left) => {
            let op = match op { ir::BinaryOp::Lt => "<", ir::BinaryOp::Lte => "<=", _ => return None };
            Some((op, print(right)?, code.clone()))
        }
        ir::Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => capacity_require(then_body, site).or_else(|| capacity_require(else_body, site)),
        _ => None,
    })
}

/// The refusal code of a `require not exists …` whose existence test reads
/// the refused page (`require not exists sightings[cat = cat] else HAS`).
fn absence_require(body: &[ir::Stmt], site: &str) -> Option<String> {
    fn reads(expr: &Expr, site: &str) -> bool {
        match expr {
            Expr::Scan(scan) => scan.site == site,
            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => reads(value, site),
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => reads(left, site) || reads(right, site),
            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().any(|arg| reads(arg, site)),
            Expr::Block { value, body } => reads(value, site) || body.iter().any(|statement| matches!(statement, ir::Stmt::Let { value, .. } if reads(value, site))),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
    body.iter().find_map(|statement| match statement {
        ir::Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Unary { op: ir::UnaryOp::Not, value }, code, .. } if reads(value, site) => Some(code.clone()),
        ir::Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => absence_require(then_body, site).or_else(|| absence_require(else_body, site)),
        _ => None,
    })
}

   146	    fn parse_use(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {

   157	                } else if self.at("guests") {

   162	                    (self.document.identity, self.document.identity_site.as_ref())

   165	                }

   929	    fn parse_rule(&mut self) -> Fallible<RuleDecl> {

   934	            "update" => Effect::Update,

   945	        } else if self.at("(") {
   946	            self.take("(")?;
   947	            bindings.push(self.binding_name()?);
   948	            while self.maybe(",") {
   949	                bindings.push(self.binding_name()?);

   963	        }

  1055	    /// `int M..N`, `int M..` or `int ..N`: inclusive bounds on the value,
  1056	    /// one spelling each. Other comparisons refuse with the canonical form.
  1057	    // @ref LLP 3000.040#12-surface — reverses LLP 3000.036 §15.7: a bounded int is a type.
  1058	    fn int_range(&mut self, site: &Site) -> Fallible<(Option<i64>, Option<i64>)> {
  1059	        let rewrite = "int 1..5, int 0.. (at least), or int ..100 (at most)";
  1060	        let bound = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<i64> {
  1061	            let negative = parser.maybe("-");
  1062	            let token = parser.peek().clone();
  1063	            if token.kind != Kind::Number {
  1064	                return Err(syntax_with(&token.site, "an int bound is an integer literal", rewrite).guide("types"));
  1065	            }
  1066	            parser.position += 1;
  1067	            if !token.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()) {
  1068	                return Err(syntax_with(&token.site, "an int bound is a whole number; fractional bounds are not allowed", rewrite).guide("types"));
  1069	            }
  1070	            let value = token.value.parse::<i64>().ok().map(|n| if negative { -n } else { n })
  1071	                .filter(|n| (snapback4_core::value::I53_MIN..=snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX).contains(n))
  1072	                .ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&token.site, "an int bound must lie within ±9007199254740991", rewrite).guide("types"))?;
  1073	            Ok(value)
  1074	        };
  1075	        let dots = |parser: &mut Self| parser.at(".") && parser.peek_at(1).value == ".";
  1076	        let at_bound = |parser: &Self| parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) || (parser.at("-") && parser.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Number);
  1077	        // One spelling: `<=N`, `>=M`, `<N` and `>N` print the canonical range.
  1078	        let operator = self.peek().value.clone();
  1079	        if matches!(operator.as_str(), "<=" | ">=" | "<" | ">") {
  1080	            self.position += 1;
  1081	            let n = bound(self)?;
  1082	            let canonical = match operator.as_str() {
  1083	                "<=" => format!("int ..{n}"),
  1084	                "<" => format!("int ..{}", n - 1),
  1085	                ">=" => format!("int {n}.."),
  1086	                _ => format!("int {}..", n + 1),
  1087	            };
  1088	            let why = if operator == "<=" { " (text's `<=N` starts at 0 because a length is never negative; an int's `..N` has no lower bound)" } else { "" };
  1089	            return Err(syntax_with(site, format!("an int range is written `M..N`, `M..` or `..N`, not `{operator}`{why}"), canonical).guide("types"));
  1090	        }
  1091	        let min = if dots(self) { None } else { Some(bound(self)?) };
  1092	        if !dots(self) {
  1093	            return Err(syntax_with(&self.peek().site.clone(), "an int range is `M..N`, `M..` or `..N`", rewrite).guide("types"));
  1094	        }
  1095	        self.position += 2;
  1096	        let max = if at_bound(self) { Some(bound(self)?) } else { None };
  1097	        match (min, max) {
  1098	            (None, None) => Err(syntax_with(site, "`int ..` bounds nothing; write `int`, or give a bound", rewrite).guide("types")),
  1099	            (Some(min), Some(max)) if min > max => Err(syntax_with(site, format!("int range minimum {min} exceeds its maximum {max}"), format!("int {max}..{min}")).guide("types")),
  1100	            range => Ok(range),
  1101	        }
  1102	    }

  1210	    fn parse_if(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {

     1	//! Programs, chunks and schedules: `query`, `mutation`, `job` (Quarry
     2	//! chunks or `native`, LLP 3000.005; a query may be `native` too, §9) and `schedule`. Split from the parser
     3	//! so each file stays under the line budget.
     4	
     5	use super::Parser;
     6	use crate::ast::*;
     7	use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, syntax, syntax_with, unsupported};
     8	use crate::lexer::Kind;
     9	use crate::print;
