   230	```
   231	
   232	`firstDay` and `lastDay` are calendar-day numbers in the app's chosen calendar;
   233	the last day is excluded. A second guest reserving an occupied night gets
   234	`TAKEN`, and the entire mutation rolls back, including any earlier free nights.
   235	The normal write and work meters bound the loop; exceeding them also rolls
   236	back the mutation. This example only books nights; cancellation must release
   237	its nights in the same transaction as cancelling the booking.
   238	
   239	The availability table is deliberately public: a named unique refusal applies
   240	only when the clashing row is readable. A hidden collision stays `E_RULE`,
   241	even with `else TAKEN`. Use this example only if occupied dates and booking
   242	references may be public. Private booking details keep their own read rule.
   243	If the product instead permits readable availability checks, an `exists` or
   244	`count` domain over that public availability table has a uniformly readable
   245	audience. Pinning a private guest merely narrows the check; it cannot enforce
   246	a constraint across all guests.
   247	
   248	## On delete restrict
   249	
   250	An undeclared reference is `on delete restrict`: deleting the parent refuses
   251	E_REFERENCED while any live child names it. Declare the product behavior on
   252	the child reference:
   253	
   254	- `on delete restrict` keeps audit/history rows and blocks the parent delete.
   255	- `on delete cascade` deletes children too; every parent group needs the
   256	  declared cap or unique key that bounds the cascade. A multi-level cascade
   257	  continues through each child's declared cascade references.
   258	- `on delete set null` clears an optional reference.
   259	- `on delete keep` preserves the child and its old parent id after deletion.
   260	
   261	For a multi-level ownership tree, use cascade only where deleting the root
   262	really deletes the descendant. Use keep or restrict at history and audit
   263	boundaries so deleting an intermediate row does not erase records that should
   264	survive.
   265	
   266	## Server-arrival order within a conversation
   267	
   268	For timestamp order with ties, use existing columns: `sync last 500 by .at, .id`
   269	and query `last 50 by .at, .id`. No ordinal or conversation-row rewrite is needed.
   270	For server-arrival order, client-minted ids and timestamps are insufficient.
   271	Declare an engine sequence. A client-supplied integer can pin
   272	the history forever by writing the largest value; `sequence by` refuses
   273	such values with E_SEQUENCE_SUPPLIED, including on updates and upserts.
   274

   278	  public 'shared conversation'
   279	  insert <- .creator = viewer

    fn parse_for(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {

    fn tuple_binding_ahead(&self) -> bool {
        let mut cursor = self.position + 1;
        if self.tokens.get(cursor).is_none_or(|t| t.kind != Kind::Word) {
            return false;
        }
        cursor += 1;
        while self.tokens.get(cursor).is_some_and(|t| t.value == ",") {
            cursor += 1;
            if self.tokens.get(cursor).is_none_or(|t| t.kind != Kind::Word) {
                return false;
            }
            cursor += 1;
        }
        self.tokens.get(cursor).is_some_and(|t| t.value == ")") && self.tokens.get(cursor + 1).is_some_and(|t| t.value == "=")
    }

    fn is_stop(&self, token: &Token, stops: &[&str]) -> bool {
        if matches!(token.kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Dedent | Kind::Eof) {
            return true;
        }
        stops.contains(&token.value.as_str())
    }

    fn end_line(&mut self, site: &Site) -> Fallible<()> {
        if !self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) {
            return Err(syntax(&self.peek().site.clone(), format!("unexpected token after declaration from line {}", site.line)));
        }
        self.newline()
    }

    fn newline(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {
        self.take_kind(Kind::Newline).map(|_| ())
    }

    fn skip_newlines(&mut self) {
        while self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) {
            self.position += 1;
        }
    }

impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
        self.take("(")?;
        let mut args = Vec::new();
        while !self.at(")") {
            let name = self.peek().clone();
            let binding = self.local_name()?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
                }
                d
            })?;
            if self.at("=") || self.at("default") {
                let at = self.peek().site.clone();
                self.position += 1;
                let fallback = self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])?;
                let mut optional = type_.clone(); optional.optional = true;
                return Err(syntax_with(&at, "operation parameters have no literal defaults; omission is null for an optional parameter",
                    format!("declare `{binding}: {}` and bind `{binding}Value = {binding} ?? {}` at the start of the body", print::scalar_type(&optional), print::expression(&fallback))).guide("types"));
            }
            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, site: name.site });
            if !self.maybe(",") {
                break;
            }
        }
        self.take(")")?;
        Ok(args)
    }

    pub personas: Vec<String>,
}

#[test]
fn reserved_words_are_named_at_their_declaration() {
    for (source, word, rename) in [
        ("table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  read: bool default false\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\n", "read", "isRead"),
        ("table notes:\n  by: principal\n  read <- .by = viewer\n  online only\n", "by", "author"),
        ("table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  after: int\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\n", "after", "afterAt"),
        ("table updates:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  delete <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\nmutation removeUpdate(update: updates):\n  delete updates[update]\n", "update", "change"),
        ("query q(x: int):\n  walk = x + 1\n  return walk\n", "walk", "outing"),
        ("query q(x: int):\n  for viewer in range(0, x):\n    return viewer\n", "viewer", "who"),
        ("query q(now: time):\n  return now\n", "now", "at"),
        ("query q():\n  now = 0\n  return now\n", "now", "at"),
        ("fn atTime(now: time): now\nquery q():\n  return atTime(0)\n", "now", "at"),
        ("mutation m():\n  commit = now\n  return commit\n", "commit", "committedAt"),
        ("mutation m(x: int):\n  for new in range(0, x):\n    return new\n", "new", "newId"),
    ] {
        let d = one(source, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED");
        assert!(d.message.contains(&format!("`{word}` is a reserved word")), "{d}");
        assert!(d.rewrite.contains(rename), "{d}");
    }
    let why = snapback4_lang::guide::explain_code("E_QUARRY_RESERVED").unwrap();
    assert!(why.contains("`read`") && why.contains("`viewer`") && why.contains("`now`") && why.contains("Rename it"), "{why}");
}

totals and private next-expiry metadata are not delivered to bypass that limit.
Live scalar misses and empty pages remain incomplete when source coverage is
incomplete. An inherited
aggregate over a public source with subset delivery runs on the server;
its local scalar and page coverage is unknown, including for the viewer.

Filtered counts disclose predicate membership, calendar groups disclose bucket
membership, and rank discloses relative values to the aggregate's read audience.
The filter grants no source-row read. N_MAINTAIN_DELTA names dependencies;
restrict the aggregate read rule when the delta is private (guide rules).
When that delta is an intended, reviewed product disclosure, record the review
without changing authority:

```quarry
maintain monthlyCounts: count escorts by zone, month(.at)
  read <- exists staff[viewer]
  disclosure reviewed 'staff intentionally see monthly zone totals'
```

The rationale silences N_MAINTAIN_DELTA for this maintain. It does not make the
source or aggregate public and does not weaken either table's write rules.

Even a hidden aggregate can disclose a constraint outcome: near an integer
endpoint, a hidden selected contribution can make a write fail with
E_OVERFLOW while an excluded contribution commits. Work reservation does not
prevent this overflow bit; the refusal names only the view and column,
and the failed transaction rolls back.

Adding or changing a filter, key, duration, offset, dependent literal default or index
is a derived rebuild at its declaration. Today's atomic activation rebuilds
populated stores before publishing the new generation; it is not resumable.
Attribution capture runs inside the same atomic activation, before that rebuild, preserving existing paths.

// Always cover the complete body; absent or malformed boundaries cannot skip
// an effect. A source statement may own several lowered top-level statements.
fn read_only_prefix(body: &[Stmt], statement_ends: &[usize]) -> (usize, usize) {
    let ends: Vec<_> = if statement_ends.is_empty() { (1..=body.len()).collect() }
        else { statement_ends.iter().copied().chain(std::iter::once(body.len())).collect() };
    let mut start = 0;
    for end in ends {
        if end <= start || end > body.len() { continue; }
        if !super::read_only::statements::<false>(&body[start..end]) { return (start, end); }
        start = end;
    }
    (body.len(), body.len())
}

struct Flow<'a> {
    schema: &'a Schema,
    program: &'a Program,
    nonuniform: Option<Scan>,
    env: BTreeMap<String, Origin>,
    error: Option<Problem>,
    bindings: BTreeMap<String, Binding>,
}

impl Flow<'_> {
    fn expr(&self, expr: &Expr) -> Origin {
        match expr {
            Expr::Block { .. } => Origin::Unknown(crate::ir::expression_scans(expr).into_iter().cloned().collect()),
            Expr::Merge(merge) => {
                Origin::Merge(merge.lanes.iter().map(|lane| lane.scan.clone()).collect())
            }
            Expr::Scan(scan) if scan.merge_site.is_some() => Origin::Merge(vec![scan.clone()]),
            Expr::Scan(scan) => Origin::Page(scan.clone()),
            Expr::Variable(name) => self
                .env
                .get(name)
                .cloned()
                .unwrap_or(Origin::Unknown(Vec::new())),
            Expr::Array(values) if values.is_empty() => Origin::Empty,
            Expr::Literal(Value::Array(values)) if values.is_empty() => Origin::Empty,
            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } | Expr::Array(args) => args
                .iter()
                .fold(Origin::Other, |origin, arg| origin.join(self.expr(arg)))
                .transformed(),
            Expr::Object(fields) | Expr::Shape { fields, .. } => fields
                .values()
                .fold(Origin::Other, |origin, value| origin.join(self.expr(value)))
                .transformed(),
            Expr::Field { base, .. } | Expr::Unary { value: base, .. } => {
                self.expr(base).transformed()
            }
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => {
                self.expr(left).join(self.expr(right)).transformed()
            }
            // A point read, list argument, or literal list has its own existing bounded contract.
            Expr::Get(_) | Expr::Exists(_) | Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Input(_) => Origin::Other,
        }
    }

    fn domain(&self, domain: &LoopDomain) -> Origin {
        match domain {
            LoopDomain::Page(scan) => self.expr(&Expr::Scan(scan.clone())),
            LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => self.expr(value),
            LoopDomain::ArgList { name, .. } => self.expr(&Expr::Variable(name.clone())),
            _ => Origin::Other,
        }
    }

    fn statements(&mut self, statements: &[Stmt], population: &Origin, top: bool) {
        for statement in statements {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Const { name, value }
                | Stmt::Let { name, value }
                | Stmt::Assign { name, value } => {
                    let origin = self.expr(value);
                    let fact = &self.bindings[name];
                    let allowed = top
                        && !self.program.args.contains_key(name)
                        && (fact.single_top() || (fact.collector() && origin == Origin::Empty));
                    let origin = if allowed {
                        origin
                    } else {
                        let old = self.env.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or(Origin::Other);
                        Origin::Unknown(old.join(origin).scans())
                    };
                    self.env.insert(name.clone(), origin);
                }
                Stmt::Push { target, value } => {
                    let old = self.env.get(target).cloned().unwrap_or(Origin::Other);
                    let origin = old.join(self.expr(value)).join(population.clone());
                    self.env
                        .insert(target.clone(), Origin::Unknown(origin.scans()));
                }
                Stmt::WriteTarget { condition: Some(condition), .. } => {
                    let before = self.env.clone();
                    self.statements(&condition.setup, population, false);
                    self.env = before;
                }
                Stmt::If {
                    then_body,
                    else_body,
                    ..
                } => {
    }

+fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,

//! Capture bare searches, then admit optional indexes against their bound plans.
//! The authored device program never acquires a membership predicate.
use super::catalog::Catalog;
use crate::ast::*;

// @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — a plain unique search order lowers to the same
// scoped words index as column order, before needle/browse variants diverge.
pub(super) fn named_order(table: &super::catalog::TableInfo, read: &mut ReadExpr) -> bool {
    if read.matching.is_none() || !read.order_keys.is_empty()
        || super::validate_key_names(read).is_err() { return false; }
    let Some(index) = table.indexes.iter().find(|index| Some(&index.name) == read.index.as_ref()) else { return false; };
    if !index.unique || !index.is_plain() || read.keys.len() > index.columns.len() { return false; }
    // Preserve the declared prefix contract, including positional arguments.
    // A named key outside that prefix must still be refused by the binder.
    if read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref().is_some_and(|column| !index.columns[..read.keys.len()].contains(column))) { return false; }
    let mut pinned: std::collections::BTreeSet<_> = read.keys.iter().enumerate()
        .map(|(n, key)| key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| index.columns[n].clone())).collect();
    let mut predicates = Vec::new();
    if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split(*filter.clone(), &mut predicates); }
    for predicate in predicates {
        if let Some((column, _)) = super::intent::equality(&predicate, table) { pinned.insert(column); }
    }
    read.order_keys = index.columns.iter().filter(|column| !pinned.contains(*column)).cloned().collect();
    if read.order_keys.is_empty() { read.order_keys.push("id".into()); }
    for (key, column) in read.keys.iter_mut().zip(&index.columns) { key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone()); }
    // Keep the authored choice until the alternatives are planned: a compatible
    // browse branch must not become ambiguous with an equivalent plain index.
    true
}

// @ref LLP 3000.028#3.3 — only a single bare ordinary search is inferred.
pub(super) fn capture(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    for doc in &catalog.documents {
        for program in &doc.programs {
            if program.kind != ProgramKind::Query || program.held || !program.prelude.is_empty() { continue; }
            let [Stmt::Return { value, .. }] = program.body.as_slice() else { continue };
            let value = match value { Expr::Shape { base, .. } | Expr::ShapeUse { base, .. } => base.as_ref(), value => value };
            let Expr::Read(read) = value else { continue };
            // Column order also identifies normalized named unique reads. An
            // explicit words index without column order still stays out.
            if read.matching.is_none() || !read.keys.is_empty()
                || read.range.is_none() || read.order_keys.is_empty()
                || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|value| !scalar(value)) { continue; }
            let Some(table) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { continue };
            if table.maintained.is_some() { continue; }
            let Some(rule) = table.decl.rules.iter().find(|r| r.effect == Effect::Read) else { continue };
            let Expr::Exists { read: probe, .. } = &rule.value else { continue };
            let Some(members) = catalog.tables.get(&probe.table) else { continue };
            if members.decl.expire.is_some() || members.maintained.is_some() { continue; }
            let module = std::path::Path::new(&doc.file).file_stem().unwrap().to_string_lossy();
            catalog.ordinary_searches.push(super::operation_name(&module, &program.name));
        }
    }
}

fn validate_arguments(schema: &Schema, program: &Program, inputs: &mut BTreeMap<String, Value>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    for (name, kind) in &program.args {
        if !inputs.contains_key(name) && (kind.is_optional() || matches!(kind, ArgType::Cursor)) {
            inputs.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
        }
        let value = inputs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input(format!("missing argument {name}")))?;
        validate_arg(schema, kind, value, name)?;
    }
    if let Some(extra) = inputs.keys().find(|name| !program.args.contains_key(*name)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{} takes no argument {extra}", program.name)));
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub fn validate_arg(schema: &Schema, kind: &ArgType, value: &Value, label: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if !kind.bounds_valid() {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} has a minimum greater than its maximum")));
    }
    crate::value::validate_identities(value)?;
    if contains_system_principal(value) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} cannot carry the reserved system principal")));
    }
    match kind {
        ArgType::Cursor => match value {
            Value::Null => Ok(()),
            Value::String(token) => crate::cursor::decode_cursor(schema, token).map(|_| ()).map_err(|error| RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label}: {error}"))),
            _ => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be an opaque cursor string or null"))),
        },
        ArgType::Optional(_) if *value == Value::Null => Ok(()),
        ArgType::Optional(inner) => validate_arg(schema, inner, value, label),
        ArgType::Scalar => match value {
            Value::Row(_) | Value::Map(_) => Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{label} must be a scalar"))),
            _ => Ok(()),
        },
        ArgType::Text { max, min, format } => match value {
//! A private population cannot answer a cross-row constraint for its writer.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#5.1 — aggregate consumers retain scan authorization and admission.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{engine::{admission, rules}, schema::{Constraint, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema}};

impl BoundProgram {
    /// The shortest repair for a hidden-row population when one direct read
    /// disjunct exposes the viewer-owned slice.
    pub(crate) fn direct_viewer_repair(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<String> {
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        let column = table.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().find_map(|rule| match rule {
            RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer)
            | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) => Some(column.as_str()),
            _ => None,
        })?;
        Some(format!(
            "First add `.{column} = viewer` to this read's `where` predicate; that directly selects the read-rule audience and is the smallest qualifying repair. "
        ))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.025#6-diagnostics-and-exact-documentation-changes — retain the admission repair before the qualified receipt-gap fallback.
    pub(crate) fn expiry_gap_diagnostic(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<Diagnostic> {
        use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
        if problem.code != "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT" || self.program.kind != ir::ProgramKind::Mutation { return None; }
        let site = problem.page_site.as_ref()?;
        let scan = ir::scans(&self.program).into_iter().find(|scan| &scan.site == site)?;
        let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
        if !table.is_expiring() || !rules::scan_visibility(schema, scan).uniform { return None; }
        let scan_index = table.index(&scan.index)?;
        let ordinal = scan_index.components.get(scan.prefix.len())?.column()?;
        if table.columns.get(ordinal) != Some(&ColumnType::Int) || !scan.orders_field(schema, ordinal) { return None; }
        let (parent, parent_table) = table.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique).find_map(|index| {
            if index.components.len() != 2 { return None; }
            let columns = [index.components[0].column()?, index.components[1].column()?];
            let parent = if columns[0] == ordinal { columns[1] } else if columns[1] == ordinal { columns[0] } else { return None; };
            let ColumnType::Ref { table: parent_table } = table.columns.get(parent)? else { return None; };
            // Consider every covering key, in either column order. An earlier
            // key on an unpinned parent cannot hide the applicable one.
            let scope = scan_index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix)
                .find_map(|(part, key)| (part.column() == Some(parent)).then(|| key.scalar()).flatten())?;
            rules::prefix_visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &[(parent, scope)].into()).uniform
                .then_some((parent, parent_table))
        })?;
        // A sender/ordinal equality may make the refused page uniform while
        // other positions in the parent group remain hidden. Never call those holes.
        // Uniformity proves one verdict for the scope, not that it is readable;
        // proving a dominating caller check needs control-flow/rule implication.
        let name = &scan.table;
        // Quarry comments keep the whole rewrite pasteable without hiding the
        // smaller repair that admits the author's original scan.
        let rewrite = format!("-- {}\n-- Fallback if the mutation must keep its prediction or effect ordering:\nfn skipExpired_{name}(scope: {parent_table}, initialPrefix: int, low: int):\n  prefix = initialPrefix\n  for i in range(0, 1024):\n    if prefix + 1 >= low:\n      break\n    if exists {name}[{parent} = scope, {ordinal} = prefix + 1]:\n      break\n    prefix = prefix + 1\n  return prefix", problem.rewrite);
        Some(Diagnostic::new(&self.sources.get(site)?.0, problem.code,
            format!("{}. For receipt gaps, try the admission repair below first. If the shape must stay refused, use bounded unique ordinal probes; a new schema can declare `{ordinal}: sequence by {parent}` with `unique {parent}, {ordinal}`. The helper's prefix justifies advancing a receipt only after the caller's own authority check (the membership check or the card's readable-message anchor). Call it inside the receipt merge, retain disjoint runs, and let an already-recorded run reach the helper again to resume after the 1,024-probe limit. For an unread badge use a row-keyed count; for general processing use a query or job (guide sequences)", problem.message),
            rewrite).guide("sequences"))
    }

    /// This is diagnostic selection only. The ordinary admission pass still

    pub(crate) fn constraint_diagnostic(&self, schema: &Schema, problem: &admission::Problem) -> Option<Diagnostic> {

/// Keep the existing scope-pinning rewrite only when a declared bounded
/// group supplies enough deciding columns. Preserve the current pins and
/// ask the engine's rule analysis, including self-probe absorption, whether
/// pinning that scope gives one verdict. A cap on unrelated columns cannot
/// turn a private conflict check into a check over every writer's rows.
fn capped_scope_decides(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> bool {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { return false };

fn usage(word: &str) -> &'static str {
    match word {
        "read" | "insert" | "update" | "delete" => "read <- rule, insert <- rule, update <- rule, delete <- rule",
        "by" | "unique" => "by name: columns, unique columns",
        "after" => "after insert <- exists …",
        "public" => "public 'why'",
        "sync" | "online" => "sync … / online only",
        "cap" => "cap N by column",
        "expire" => "expire at .column",
        "not" => "not before .column",
        "immutable" => "immutable columns",
        "retain" => "retain until-revoked",
        "ephemeral" => "ephemeral 5s",
        "search" => "search column",
        _ => "a table line",
    }
}

fn non_null_term(left: &RuleTerm, right: &RuleTerm) -> Vec<RuleTerm> {
    match (left, right) {
        (RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null), term) | (term, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null)) => vec![term.clone()],
        _ => Vec::new(),
    }
}

fn rule_expr(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, expr: &Expr, bindings: &RuleBindings) -> Fallible<RuleExpr> {
    let site = expr.site();
    match expr {
        Expr::Name { name, .. } => match name.as_str() {
            "allow" => Ok(RuleExpr::Allow),
            "deny" => Ok(RuleExpr::Deny),
            "public" => Ok(RuleExpr::Public { reason: format!("{} is public", info.decl.name) }),
            "true" => Ok(RuleExpr::Allow),
            "false" => Ok(RuleExpr::Deny),
            other => Err(unknown(site, format!("`{other}` is not a rule; rules are allow, deny, public, exists, created, and comparisons over ., viewer and literals"), "read <- .ownerId = viewer")),
        },
        Expr::Call { name, args, .. } if name == "public" => {
            let reason = match args.first() {
                Some(Expr::Literal { value: crate::ast::Literal::Str(reason), .. }) => reason.clone(),
                _ => format!("{} is public", info.decl.name),
            };
            Ok(RuleExpr::Public { reason })
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } => match op.as_str() {
            "and" | "or" => {
                let left = rule_expr(catalog, info, left, bindings)?;
                let mut guarded = bindings.clone();
                guarded.present.extend(present_terms(&left, op == "and"));
                let right = rule_expr(catalog, info, right, &guarded)?;
                Ok(if op == "and" { RuleExpr::And(vec![left, right]) } else { RuleExpr::Or(vec![left, right]) })
            }
            "=" | "!=" | ">=" | ">" | "<=" | "<" => {
                let ordered = !matches!(op.as_str(), "=" | "!=");
                let transition = bindings.update && bindings.old.is_some();
                let effect = super::rule_operator::effect_word(bindings.insert, bindings.update, !bindings.hops);
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — unsupported terms and misplaced ordered comparisons name what rules evaluate and move the author's comparison into the mutation.
                if let Some(what) = [left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()].into_iter().find_map(super::rule_operator::unsupported_side) {
                    return Err(super::rule_operator::diagnostic(site, &info.decl.name, effect, transition, expr, what));
                }
                if ordered && !transition && !bindings.insert { return Err(super::rule_operator::diagnostic(site, &info.decl.name, effect, transition, expr, "an ordered comparison")); }
                let (mut l, mut lt) = rule_term(catalog, info, left, bindings)?;
                let (mut r, mut rt) = rule_term(catalog, info, right, bindings)?;
                // `viewer = 'service:ranker'`: a text literal beside the
                // viewer names a principal, as it does beside `=` in a program.
                let as_principal = |term: &mut RuleTerm, ty: &mut Ty| {
                    if let RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(text)) = term {
                        *term = RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone()));
                        *ty = Ty::Principal;
                    }
                };
                if matches!(l, RuleTerm::Viewer) {
                    as_principal(&mut r, &mut rt);
                }
                if matches!(r, RuleTerm::Viewer) {
                    as_principal(&mut l, &mut lt);
                }
                if !lt.comparable(&rt) {
                    return Err(type_error(site, format!("rule comparison joins {} with {}", lt.name(), rt.name()), "compare a principal column with viewer, or a column with a literal of its own type"));
                }
                if let (Ty::Principal, other) | (other, Ty::Principal) = (&lt, &rt)
                    && !matches!(other, Ty::Principal | Ty::Id)
                    && !matches!((&l, &r), (RuleTerm::Viewer, _) | (_, RuleTerm::Viewer))
                {

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

- [rule_operator.rs:74](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/rule_operator.rs:74): `named_update_source` rewrites printed text through `map_fields`, which does not skip quoted literals. For example, `old.label = '.owner'` becomes `old.label = 'next.owner'`. With a text column, this compiles while changing the authored authorization predicate. Rewrite AST field nodes or preserve quoted strings; add a literal-preservation regression.

- [readiness.rs:157](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/readiness.rs:157): Refusal suppression also hides malformed case structure. `{"op":"answer","args":false,"expect":"denied"}` loses N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT, although the runner rejects non-object arguments during case validation unless the expectation is specifically E_INPUT. Validate structure before suppressing argument-domain failures and cover this boundary.

- [wave10_diagnostics.rs:17](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/check/wave10_diagnostics.rs:17): The added NOT_ALLOWED case supplies `n:6` to `int 1..5`, which returns E_INPUT. Exact codes must match, so the success assertion at line 24 fails. Separate check-only suppression coverage from executable cases, or provide an operation that actually returns NOT_ALLOWED.

- [wave12_diagnostics.rs:132](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/tests/wave12_diagnostics.rs:132): The assertion expects `.organizer = viewer`, but [constraints.rs:14](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/constraints.rs:14) selects the first direct disjunct. This fixture declares `.member = viewer` first, so the emitted prefix contradicts the assertion. Resolve the selection/assertion mismatch and compile the emitted repair.

- [positional_keys.rs:33](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/positional_keys.rs:33): A foreign reference is classified as an own-id mismatch even when compatible secondary keys exist. If `rolls` has two unique `secrets` reference columns, `rolls[secret]` is ambiguous between those keys; the new diagnostic incorrectly says another table’s reference cannot select `rolls` and recommends a rolls id. Preserve compatible-key ambiguity advice; reserve this mismatch wording for an actual incompatible key.

- [auth_lints.rs:744](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/auth_lints.rs:744): Substring matching treats negative or conditional facts as established readable rows. A preceding `require not exists notes[note] else PRESENT` contains `exists notes[` and therefore makes a later gatecodes probe claim that a readable notes row was established. Require positive, dominating existence evidence; add negated and disjunctive controls.

- [constraints.rs:10](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r1a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/constraints.rs:10): Direct-viewer advice is applied to every mutation E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT with a direct read disjunct, including expiry refusals on already-uniform scans after a write. Adding the existing viewer predicate cannot repair that refusal, yet the prefix calls it qualifying. Gate the advice on the relevant visibility failure and verify the proposed predicate resolves it; retain expiry-specific advice otherwise.
